\| W - \underline {{W}} \| _ {t} \leq K _ {T} \left((1 + B) \int_ {0} ^ {t} \| W - \underline {{W}} \| _ {s} d s + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right) + \| W - \underline {{W}} \| _ {0}.
$$

Hence Gronwall's lemma yields:

$$
\left\| W - \underline {{W}} \right\| _ {T} \leq \left(\left\| W - \underline {{W}} \right\| _ {0} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right) e ^ {K _ {T} (1 + B)}.
$$

Notice that for  $2 \leq i \leq L$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) - \underline {{w}} _ {i} \left(0, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) - B \right| ^ {2} \mathbb {I} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| > B\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} \mathbb {I} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| > B\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {4} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| > B\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq K e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last displays comes from Assumption 5 and in particular we have [Ver10]:

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| \geq r\right) \leq K e ^ {- K r ^ {2}}, \quad \forall r \geq 0.
$$

Similarly,

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right) - \underline {{b}} _ {i} \left(0, C _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] \leq K e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}.
$$

Also recall that  $\underline{w}_1(0,c_1) = w_1^0 (c_1)$ . As such, a similar bound holds for  $\| W - \underline{W}\| _0$  and this gives the desired bound on  $\| W - \underline{W}\| _T$ .

The derivation for  $\left\| \tilde{W} - \underline{\tilde{W}} \right\|_T$  is similar. Indeed Lemma 51 indicates that for any fixed  $r \geq 0$ , with probability at least  $1 - KLn_{\mathrm{max}} \exp \left(-Ke^{-Kr^2}n_{\mathrm{min}}^{1/52}\right)$ , we have:

$$
\| \tilde {W} \| _ {0} \leq \| W \| _ {0} + e ^ {- K r ^ {2}} \leq K,
$$

as well as that for all  $i\in \{2,\dots ,L\}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n _ {i - 1} n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i - 1} = 1} ^ {n _ {i - 1}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {I} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1} (j _ {i - 1}), C _ {i} (j _ {i})\right) \right| \geq r\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\left| w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \right| \geq r\right) + e ^ {- K r ^ {2}} \leq K e ^ {- K r ^ {2}}, \\ \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {I} \left(\left| b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i} (j _ {i})\right) \right| \geq r\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\left| b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}) \right| \geq r\right) + e ^ {- K r ^ {2}} \leq K e ^ {- K r ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By taking  $r = B$  and performing an argument similar to the bounding of  $\| W - \underline{W} \|_T$ , we obtain:

$$
\left\| \tilde {W} - \underline {{\tilde {W}}} \right\| _ {T} \leq \left(\left\| \tilde {W} - \underline {{\tilde {W}}} \right\| _ {0} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right) e ^ {K _ {T} (1 + B)} \leq K e ^ {- K B ^ {2} + K _ {T} (1 + B)},
$$

with probability at least  $1 - KLn_{\mathrm{max}}\exp \left(-Ke^{-KB^2}n_{\mathrm{min}}^{1 / 52}\right)$ . The derivation for  $\| \mathbf{W} - \underline{\mathbf{W}}\| _T$  is also similar.

□

# C.2 Proofs of Corollaries 17, 18 and 19

Lemma 53. Consider the MF trajectory  $W(t)$ ,  $t \leq T$ , under Assumptions 1-3 and 5. For any  $\zeta \geq 0$ ,  $\| W - W_{\zeta} \|_T \leq K_{T + \zeta} \zeta$ , where  $W_{\zeta}(t) = W(t + \zeta)$  and  $K_{T + \zeta}$  is a finite constant that depends on the initialization  $W(0)$  and grows continuously with  $\zeta$ .

Proof. We reuse the notation  $K_{t}$  from the proof of Proposition 22. By Assumption 3 and Lemma 52, for  $2 \leq i \leq L$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \sup  _ {t \leq T + \zeta} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (t, Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \right] \leq K \Bigg (1 + \mathbb {E} \left[ \sup  _ {t \leq T + \zeta} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {H} (t, Z, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \right] \Bigg) \leq K _ {T + \zeta},
$$

and therefore, by Assumption 1,

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \sup _ {t \leq T} \left| w _ {i} (t + \zeta , C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) - w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq K _ {T + \zeta} \zeta .
$$

One can also deduce a similar bound for  $b_{i}$  and  $w_{1}$ .

Proof of Corollary 17. We reuse the notation  $K_{t}$  from the proof of Proposition 22. We have the following decomposition for  $i \in [L]$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(\mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, j _ {i}\right)\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} (t, X, C _ {i})\right) \right] \right| \\ \leq \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} | \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(\mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, j _ {i}\right)\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right)\right) \right] | \\ + \left| \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i} (j _ {i}))\right)\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i}\right)\right) \right] \right| \\ + \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ | \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i}\right)\right) - \psi \left(H _ {i} (t, X, C _ {i})\right) | \right] \\ \leq K \left(\frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ | \mathbf {H} _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, j _ {i}) - H _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i} (j _ {i})) | ^ {2} \right]\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ + \left| \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i} (j _ {i})\right)\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i}\right)\right) \right] \right| \\ + K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \left| H _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, C _ {i}) - H _ {i} (t, X, C _ {i}) \right| \right] \\ = Q _ {1, i} (t) + Q _ {2, i} (t) + Q _ {3, i} (t), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use the fact  $\psi$  is  $K$ -Lipschitz and Cauchy-Schwarz's inequality. We provide bounds on each term. Note that by the fact  $\psi$  is  $K$ -Lipschitz and Assumption 2:

$$
{| \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \psi (Y, \hat {\mathbf {y}} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X)) ] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \psi (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) ] | \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ | \mathbf {H} _ {L} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, 1) - H _ {L} (t, X, 1) | ],}
$$

and as such, bounding  $Q_{1,L}$  gives the last claim in the corollary.

Bounding  $Q_{1,i}$ . By Assumption 2 and Cauchy-Schwarz's inequality, for  $i \geq 2$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| Q _ {1, i} (t) \right| ^ {2} \leq \frac {K}{n _ {i - 1} n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i - 1} = 1} ^ {n _ {i - 1}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \left(1 + \left| \mathbf {w} _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i - 1}, j _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1} (j _ {i - 1}), C _ {i} (j _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right. \\ \left. + \left| \mathbf {b} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i} \left(t, C _ {i} (j _ {i})\right) \right| ^ {2}\right) \left| Q _ {1, i - 1} (t) \right| ^ {2} \\ + K \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W}) \\ \leq \frac {K}{n _ {i - 1} n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i - 1} = 1} ^ {n _ {i - 1}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \left(1 + | w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1} (j _ {i - 1}), C _ {i} (j _ {i})) | ^ {2} + | b _ {i} (t, C _ {i} (j _ {i})) | ^ {2} + \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W})\right) | Q _ {1, i - 1} (t) | ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} + K \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W}) \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} K _ {T} \left(1 + \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W})\right) | Q _ {1, i - 1} (t) | ^ {2} + K \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W}) \\ \leq K _ {T} \left(1 + \mathcal {D} _ {t} ^ {2} (W, \mathbf {W})\right) | Q _ {1, i - 1} (t) | ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(a)$  holds for all  $t\leq T$  and all  $i\geq 2$  with probability at least  $1 - KLn_{\mathrm{max}}\exp \left(-Kn_{\mathrm{min}}^{1 / 52}\right)$ , by Lemmas 51 and 52. Also from Assumption 2:  $Q_{1,1}(t)\leq K\mathcal{D}_t(W,\mathbf{W})$ . We thus have:

$$
\max  _ {i \in [ L ]} \sup  _ {t \leq T} Q _ {1, i} (t) \leq K _ {T} \left(1 + \mathcal {D} _ {T} ^ {L} \left(W, \mathbf {W}\right)\right) \mathcal {D} _ {T} \left(W, \mathbf {W}\right).
$$

with probability at least  $1 - KLn_{\mathrm{max}}\exp \left(-Kn_{\mathrm{min}}^{1 / 52}\right)$

Bounding  $Q_{2,i}$ . Recall that  $\{C_i(j_i)\}_{j_i\in [n_i]}$  are  $\eta_{i}$ -independent and  $\eta_{i} \leq n_{i}^{-1 / 2}$ . Since  $\psi$  is  $K$ -bounded, we have by Theorem 44 and the union bound that

$$
\sup  _ {t \leq T} Q _ {2, i} (t) \leq \sqrt {\frac {K}{n _ {i}} \log \left(\frac {K T}{\epsilon \delta}\right)}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ .

Bounding  $Q_{3,i}$ . By Assumption 2 and Lemma 52, for all  $t \leq T$  and  $i \geq 2$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| Q _ {3, i} (t) \right| ^ {2} \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ 1 + \left| w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i} (t, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| w _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \left| Q _ {3, i - 1} (t) \right| ^ {2} \\ + K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) - w _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i} (t, C _ {i}) - b _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq K _ {T} \left| Q _ {3, i - 1} (t) \right| ^ {2} + K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) - w _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i} (t, C _ {i}) - b _ {i} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly,

$$
\left| Q _ {3, 1} (t) \right| ^ {2} \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}) - w _ {1} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , C _ {1}) \right| ^ {2} \right].
$$

We thus obtain from Lemma 53:

$$
\max_{i\in [L]} \sup_{t\leq T}Q_{3,i}\left(t\right)\leq K_{T}\epsilon .
$$

Putting together. All previous bounds show that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {t \leq T} \left| \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \psi \left(\mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, j _ {i}\right)\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \psi \left(H _ {i} (t, X, C _ {i})\right) \right] \right| \\ \leq K _ {T} \left(1 + \mathcal {D} _ {T} ^ {L} (W, \mathbf {W})\right) \mathcal {D} _ {T} (W, \mathbf {W}) + \sqrt {\frac {K}{n _ {i}} \log \left(\frac {K T}{\epsilon \delta}\right)} + K _ {T} \epsilon \\ \end{array}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \delta - KLn_{\max} \exp \left(-Kn_{\min}^{1/52}\right)$ . Together with Theorem 15, we obtain the claim.

Proof of Corollary 18. In the following, for a set  $J = \{N_1, \dots, N_L\}$  with  $N_L = 1$ , we write  $J \to \infty$  to mean that  $N_1, \dots, N_{L-1} \to \infty$  such that for  $N_{\mathrm{max}} = \max J$  and  $N_{\mathrm{min}} = \min \{N_1, \dots, N_{L-1}\}$ , we have  $N_{\mathrm{min}} \to \infty$  and  $N_{\mathrm{min}}^{-c} \log N_{\mathrm{max}} \to 0$  for any  $c > 0$ .

For a given  $T \geq 0$  and a set of integers  $I = \{n_1, \dots, n_L\}$ , for any two sets  $\mathcal{W}^{(1)}$  and  $\mathcal{W}^{(2)}$  of the form

$$
\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)} = \left\{w _ {1} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {1}), w _ {i} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {i - 1}, r _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {i}): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ], t \in [ 0, T ] \right\},
$$

and similar for  $\mathcal{W}^{(2)}$ , let us equip a distance metric:

$$
d _ {I, T} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right) = \max  \left(\max  _ {1 \leq i \leq L} d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right), \max  _ {2 \leq i \leq L} d _ {I, T} ^ {b, i} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right)\right),
$$

$$
d _ {I, T} ^ {w, 1} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right) = \left(\frac {1}{n _ {1}} \sum_ {r _ {1} = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \sup  _ {t \leq T} \left| w _ {1} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {1}) - w _ {1} ^ {(2)} (t, r _ {1}) \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2},
$$

$$
d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right) = \left(\frac {1}{n _ {i - 1} n _ {i}} \sum_ {r _ {i - 1} = 1} ^ {n _ {i - 1}} \sum_ {r _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \sup _ {t \leq T} \left| w _ {i} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {i - 1}, r _ {i}) - w _ {i} ^ {(2)} (t, r _ {i - 1}, r _ {i}) \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2},
$$

$$
d _ {I, T} ^ {b, i} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right) = \left(\frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {r _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \sup _ {t \leq T} \left| b _ {i} ^ {(1)} (t, r _ {i}) - b _ {i} ^ {(2)} (t, r _ {i}) \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}, \qquad 2 \leq i \leq L.
$$

Let us also consider the space  $\mathcal{F}_{I,T}$  of 1-bounded Lipschitz functions  $f$  w.r.t. to this distance metric:

$$
\left| f \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}\right) - f \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right) \right| \leq 2 \wedge d _ {I, T} \left(\mathcal {W} ^ {(1)}, \mathcal {W} ^ {(2)}\right).
$$

Step 1: Coupling via finite-width networks. Recall that  $\left(\Omega, P, \{w_i^0\}_{i \in [L]}, \{b_i^0\}_{2 \leq i \leq L}\right)$  satisfies Assumption 4, i.e.,  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence. As such, for each index  $J = \{N_1, \dots, N_L\}$  of Init, one can find a sampling rule for which the samples  $\{C_i(j_i)\}_{j_i \in [N_i]}$  are  $\eta_i$ -independent for  $i \leq L-1$  and  $\eta_i \to 0$  as  $N_i \to \infty$ . Then one obtains a neural network initialization  $\mathbf{W}(0)$  with law  $\rho$  by setting

$$
\mathbf {w} _ {1} \left(0, j _ {1}\right) = w _ {1} \left(0, C _ {1} (j _ {1})\right), \mathbf {w} _ {i} \left(0, j _ {i - 1}, j _ {i}\right) = w _ {i} \left(0, C _ {i - 1} \left(j _ {i - 1}\right), C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right),
$$

$$
\mathbf {b} _ {i} \left(0, j _ {i}\right) = b _ {i} \left(0, C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right), \quad 2 \leq i \leq L.
$$

Similarly using  $\left(\hat{\Omega},\hat{P},\left\{\hat{w}_i^0\right\}_{i\in [L]},\left\{\hat{b}_i^0\right\}_{2\leq i\leq L}\right)$  we obtain  $\hat{\mathbf{W}} (0)$  with the same law  $\rho$  by setting

$$
\hat {\mathbf {w}} _ {1} \left(0, j _ {1}\right) = \hat {w} _ {1} \left(0, \hat {C} _ {1} (j _ {1})\right), \hat {\mathbf {w}} _ {i} \left(0, j _ {i - 1}, j _ {i}\right) = \hat {w} _ {i} \left(0, \hat {C} _ {i - 1} \left(j _ {i - 1}\right), \hat {C} _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right),
$$

$$
\hat {\mathbf {b}} _ {i} \left(0, j _ {i}\right) = \hat {b} _ {i} \left(0, \hat {C} _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right), \quad 2 \leq i \leq L,
$$

where  $\left\{\hat{C}_i(j_i)\right\}_{j_i\in [N_i]}$  are  $\eta_{i}$ -independent for  $i\leq L - 1$ . We consider the evolution  $\mathbf{W}(t)$  starting from  $\mathbf{W}(0)$  (which is independent of  $W$  once  $\mathbf{W}(0)$  is fixed). Note that  $\mathbf{W}(t)$  is a deterministic function of its initialization  $\mathbf{W}(0)$  and the data  $\{z(s)\}_{s\leq t}$ . Similarly, we consider the counterpart for  $\hat{W}$ : the evolution  $\hat{\mathbf{W}}(t)$  as a function of the initialization  $\hat{\mathbf{W}}(0)$  and the data  $\{\hat{z}(s)\}_{s\leq t}$ . Due to sharing the same distribution for both the initialization and the data, these evolutions have the same law. In other words, for any  $\theta >0$ ,

$$
\inf_{\substack{\text{coupling of} (\mathbf{W},\hat{\mathbf{W}})}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\| \mathbf{W} - \hat{\mathbf{W}}\right\|_{T}\geq \theta\right) = 0,
$$

in which

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {W} - \hat {\mathbf {W}} \right\| _ {T} = \max  \left(\left(\frac {1}{N _ {1}} \sum_ {j _ {1} = 1} ^ {N _ {1}} \sup  _ {t \leq T} | \mathbf {w} _ {1} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {1}) - \hat {\mathbf {w}} _ {1} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {1}) | ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}, \right. \\ \max  _ {2 \leq i \leq L} \left(\frac {1}{N _ {i - 1} N _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i - 1} = 1} ^ {N _ {i - 1}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {N _ {i}} \sup  _ {t \leq T} \left| \mathbf {w} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i - 1}, j _ {i}\right) - \hat {\mathbf {w}} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i - 1}, j _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}, \\ \max  _ {2 \leq i \leq L} \left(\frac {1}{N _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {N _ {i}} \sup  _ {t \leq T} \left| \mathbf {b} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i}\right) - \hat {\mathbf {b}} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , j _ {i}\right)\right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\left. \right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Theorem 15 implies that following the coupling procedure, for any  $\delta >0$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta - o_{J;L}$ ,

$$
\mathscr {D} _ {T} (W, \mathbf {W}) \leq o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L},
$$

where here and in the following, we denote by  $o_{J;L}$  and  $o_{\epsilon,J;\delta,T,L}$  appropriate quantities that may change from line to line with  $o_{J;L} \to 0$  and  $o_{\epsilon,J;\delta,T,L} \to 0$  as the learning rate  $\epsilon \to 0$  and  $J \to \infty$ . Here without loss of generality, we assume  $o_{\epsilon,J;\delta,T,L} > 0$ . We also have a similar result for  $\mathcal{D}_T(\hat{W},\hat{\mathbf{W}})$ . As such,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \inf  _ {\text {c o u p l i n g o f} (\mathcal {W}, \hat {\mathcal {W}})} \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {J, T} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \geq o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {D} _ {T} (W, \mathbf {W}) \geq o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {D} _ {T} (\hat {W}, \hat {\mathbf {W}}) \geq o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L}\right) \\ + \inf  _ {\text {c o u p l i n g o f} (\mathbf {W}, \hat {\mathbf {W}})} \mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \mathbf {W} - \hat {\mathbf {W}} \right\| _ {T} \geq o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L}\right) \\ \leq 2 \delta + 2 o _ {J; L}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we define

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {W} _ {J, T} = \left\{w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} \left(j _ {1}\right)\right), w _ {i} \left(t, C _ {i - 1} \left(j _ {i - 1}\right), C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right), b _ {i} \left(t, C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right): j _ {i} \in [ N _ {i} ], i \in [ L ], t \in [ 0, T ] \right\}, \\ \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T} = \Big \{\hat {w} _ {1} \left(t, \hat {C} _ {1} \left(j _ {1}\right)\right), \hat {w} _ {i} \left(t, \hat {C} _ {i - 1} \left(j _ {i - 1}\right), \hat {C} _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right), \hat {b} _ {i} \left(t, \hat {C} _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right): j _ {i} \in [ N _ {i} ], i \in [ L ], t \in [ 0, T ] \Big \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

This gives a sense of approximate closeness between  $W$  and  $\hat{W}$  on a set  $J = \{N_1, \dots, N_L\}$  with sufficiently large size  $N_i$ , importantly under the assumption of  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence. To extend this to arbitrary finite sizes, we perform the following argument.

Step 2: Extension to finite sizes. For a given fixed set  $I = \{n_1, \dots, n_L\}$  with  $n_L = 1$ , let us consider the following sub-sampling procedure: for each  $i \in [L]$  and each  $r_i \in [n_i]$ , we independently sample  $V_i(r_i)$  uniformly from  $[N_i]$ , and then set  $S_i(r_i) = C_i(V_i(r_i))$  and  $\hat{S}_i(r_i) = \hat{C}_i(V_i(r_i))$ . Let us define

$$
\mathcal {W} _ {I, T} = \left\{w _ {1} \left(t, S _ {1} \left(r _ {1}\right)\right), w _ {i} \left(t, S _ {i - 1} \left(r _ {i - 1}\right), S _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right)\right), b _ {i} \left(t, S _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right)\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ], t \in [ 0, T ] \right\},
$$

and  $\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{I,T}$  similarly. We prove that  $\mathrm{Law}(\mathcal{W}_{I,T})$  and  $\mathrm{Law}(\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{I,T})$  are close in an appropriate sense. This shall be done via a connection with  $d_{J,T}\left(\mathcal{W}_{J,T},\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{J,T}\right)$  on the set  $J$ .

Let  $\mathbb{E}_V$  denote the expectation w.r.t. the sub-sampling procedure only (i.e. w.r.t. the randomness of  $\{V_i(r_i):r_i\in [n_i],i\in [L]\}$ ). Notice that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {V} \left[ \left| d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] = \left| d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \right| ^ {2}, \\ \mathbb {E} _ {V} \left[ \left| d _ {I, T} ^ {b, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] = \left| d _ {J, T} ^ {b, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \right| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Using this fact and Markov's inequality, for any  $\theta > 0$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \geq \theta\right) \right. \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} (\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}) \geq \theta^ {2}\right) + \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {V} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left(d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} (\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}) \geq \theta\right) \right] \mathbb {I} \left(d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} (\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}) \leq \theta^ {2}\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \geq \theta^ {2}\right) + \theta^ {- 2} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {V} \left[ \left| d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] \mathbb {I} \left(d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \leq \theta^ {2}\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {J, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {J, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {J, T}\right) \geq \theta^ {2}\right) + \theta^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The bound on  $d_{J,T}\left(\mathcal{W}_{J,T},\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{J,T}\right)$  gives:

$$
\inf_{\substack{\text{coupling of} (\mathcal{W},\hat{\mathcal{W}})}}\mathbb{P}\left(d^{w,i}_{J,T}\left(\mathcal{W}_{J,T},\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{J,T}\right)\geq \tilde{O}_{\epsilon ,J;\delta ,T,L}(1)\right)\leq 2\delta +2o_{J;L}.
$$

Then by taking  $\theta = \sqrt{o_{\epsilon,J;\delta,T,L}}$ , we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \inf  _ {\mathrm {c o u p l i n g o f} (\mathcal {W}, \hat {\mathcal {W}})} \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {I, T} ^ {w, i} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \geq \sqrt {o _ {\epsilon , J ; \delta , T , L}}\right) \leq e _ {T, L} (\delta , \epsilon , J), \\ e _ {T, L} (\delta , \epsilon , J) = 2 \delta + 2 o _ {J; L} + o _ {\epsilon , J; \delta , T, L}. \\ \end{array}
$$

A similar fact holds for  $d_{I,T}^{b,i}$ , and therefore by the union bound,

$$
\inf  _ {\mathrm {c o u p l i n g o f} (\mathcal {W}, \hat {\mathcal {W}})} \mathbb {P} \left(d _ {I, T} \left(\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}, \hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}\right) \geq \sqrt {o _ {\epsilon , J ; \delta , T , L} (\epsilon , J)}\right) \leq 2 L e _ {T, L} (\delta , \epsilon , J).
$$

In particular, this implies for any  $f \in \mathcal{F}_{I,T}$ :

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} [ f (\mathcal {W} _ {I, T}) ] - \mathbb {E} [ f (\hat {\mathcal {W}} _ {I, T}) ] \right| \leq 4 L e _ {T, L} (\delta , \epsilon , J) + \sqrt {\partial_ {\epsilon , J ; \delta , T , L}}.
$$

This describes closeness between  $\mathrm{Law}(\mathcal{W}_{I,T})$  and  $\mathrm{Law}(\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{I,T})$ . Note that this is not sufficient to conclude the proof (via taking  $\epsilon \to 0$ ,  $J \to \infty$ ,  $\delta \to 0$ ): the left-hand side involves the random variables  $C_i(V_i(r_i))$ , which firstly does not remove  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence and secondly is not independent of  $J$  since  $V_i(r_i) \in [N_i]$ .

Step 3: Removing  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence. Let  $\{U_i(j_i)\}_{j_i\in [N_i]}$  be drawn i.i.d. from  $P_{i}$ , independently for each  $i\in [L]$ , as in the statement of the corollary. We recall that  $\{C_i(j_i)\}_{j_i\in [N_i]}$  are  $\eta_{i}$ -independent for  $i\leq L - 1$  with  $\eta_{i}\to 0$  as  $N_{i}\to \infty$ . We also note  $N_{L} = n_{L} = 1$  and hence  $U_{L}(1) = C_{L}(1)$ . As such, for any 1-bounded function  $g$ :

$$
\left. \right.\left| \mathbb {E} _ {\sim V} \left[ g \left(S _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ]\right)\right] - \mathbb {E} _ {\sim V} \left[ g \left(U _ {i} \left(V _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right)\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ]\right)\right]\right| \leq o _ {J; L, I},
$$

where  $o_{J;L,I}$  is a deterministic quantity such that  $o_{J;L,I} \to 0$  as  $J \to \infty$  and  $\mathbb{E}_{\sim V}$  denotes the expectation w.r.t. everything excluding the sub-sampling procedure. Indeed supposing that  $\left\{\tilde{V}_1(1), \dots, \tilde{V}_1(n_1)\right\}$  is a permutation of  $\{V_1(1), \dots, V_1(n_1)\}$  such that  $\tilde{V}_1(1) > \dots > \tilde{V}_1(n_1)$ . Using the  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence property, we have the following for  $|\zeta_1| \leq \eta_1$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\sim V} \left[ g \left(S _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ]\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\sim V} \mathbb {E} _ {\sim C _ {1} (\tilde {V} _ {1} (1))} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1} (\tilde {V} _ {1} (1)) | \sim C _ {1} (\tilde {V} _ {1} (1))} \left[ g \left(C _ {1} (\tilde {V} _ {1} (r _ {1}))  ,   C _ {i} \left(V _ {i} (r _ {i})\right):   r _ {1} \in [ n _ {1} ],   r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ]     \text {f o r} i \geq 2\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb{E}_{\sim V}\mathbb{E}_{\sim C_{1}\big(\tilde{V}_{1}(1)\big)}\mathbb{E}_{U_{1}\big(\tilde{V}_{1}(1)\big)}\left[g\left(U_{1}\left(\tilde{V}_{1}\left(1\right)\right),C_{1}\left(\tilde{V}_{1}\left(r_{1}\right)\right),C_{i}\left(V_{i}\left(r_{i}\right)\right):r_{1}\in [n_{1}]\setminus \{1\} ,r_{i}\in [n_{i}]\text{for} i\geq 2\right)\right] + \zeta_{1}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where conditioning on the sub-sampling,  $\mathbb{E}_{\sim C_1(\tilde{V}_1(1))}$  is the expectation w.r.t. everything excluding  $C_1(\tilde{V}_1(1))$ , and  $\mathbb{E}_{C_1(\tilde{V}_1(1))|\sim C_1(\tilde{V}_1(1))}$  is w.r.t.  $C_1(\tilde{V}_1(1))$  conditioning on everything else. (Here we have assumed that  $V_{1}(1),\dots,V_{1}(n_{1})$  are all distinct, since any repeated elements can be removed without affecting the argument.) By iterating this decomposition, we obtain the claim.

On the other hand, since  $\{U_i(j_i)\}_{j_i\in [N_i]}$  are i.i.d., it is easy to see that

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ g \left(U _ {i} \left(V _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right)\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ]\right) \right] = \mathbb {E} \left[ g \left(U _ {i} \left(r _ {i}\right): r _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ]\right) \right].
$$

Together with the result from the previous step, we thus have for any  $f\in \mathcal{F}_{I,T}$

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} [ f (\mathcal {W} (I, T)) ] - \mathbb {E} [ f (\hat {\mathcal {W}} (I, T)) ] \right| \leq 2 o _ {J; L, I} + 8 L e _ {T, L} (\delta , \epsilon , J) + 2 \sqrt {o _ {\epsilon , J ; \delta , T , L}},
$$

where we recall

$$
\mathcal {W} (I, T) = \left\{w _ {1} \left(t, U _ {1} \left(j _ {1}\right)\right), w _ {i} \left(t, U _ {i - 1} \left(j _ {i - 1}\right), U _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right), b _ {i} \left(t, U _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right)\right): j _ {i} \in [ n _ {i} ], i \in [ L ], t \in [ 0, T ] \right\},
$$

and similarly for  $\hat{\mathcal{W}}(I, T)$ . Note that the left-hand side is completely independent of  $J$ ,  $\epsilon$  and  $\delta$ . So by taking  $\epsilon \to 0$ ,  $J \to \infty$ ,  $\delta \to 0$ , we have:

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ f (\mathcal {W} (I, T)) \right] = \mathbb {E} \left[ f (\hat {\mathcal {W}} (I, T)) \right],
$$

which completes the proof.

![](images/266838a91348473de5b670c311a368daa0fa0f3098a53216fe495484c01d2edd.jpg)

Proof of Corollary 19. For any test function  $\psi : \mathbb{W}_1 \times \mathbb{W}_2 \to \mathbb{R}$  that is bounded with bounded gradient, we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {d}{d t} \int \psi (u _ {1}, u _ {2}) d \rho_ {t} (u _ {1}, u _ {2}) = \frac {d}{d t} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} [ \psi (w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}), w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1)) ] \\ = - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \left\langle \nabla_ {1} \psi \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}), w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1)\right), \xi_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {1} ^ {w} (t, Z, C _ {1}) \right] \right\rangle \right] \\ - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \left\langle \nabla_ {2} \psi \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}), w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1)\right), \xi_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {2} ^ {w} (t, Z, C _ {1}, 1) \right] \right\rangle \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(a)} {=} - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \left\langle \nabla_ {1} \psi \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}), w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1)\right), \xi_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \underline {{\Delta}} _ {1} ^ {w} \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}; Z, \rho_ {t}\right) \right] \right\rangle \right] \\ - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \left\langle \nabla_ {2} \psi \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}), w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1)\right), \xi_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \underline {{\Delta}} _ {2} ^ {w} \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}; Z, \rho_ {t}\right) \right] \right\rangle \right] \\ = - \int \left\langle \nabla \psi \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}\right), G \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}; \rho_ {t}\right) \right\rangle d \rho_ {t} \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where step  $(a)$  can be checked easily by inspection. This shows that  $\rho_{t}$  satisfies the claimed distributional partial differential equation. The rest of the claims follow in a similar vein to the proof of Corollary 17.  $\square$

# D Remaining details for Section 5

# D.1 Infinite- $M$  limit of the canonical MF limit under i.i.d. initializations

We give the full description of the infinite- $M$  limit  $W^{*}$  of the canonical MF limit, described in Section 5.1.2. To that end, let us first consider depth  $L \geq 5$ . Let  $\{w_{i}^{*}\}_{i=1}^{L}$  and  $\{b_{i}^{*}\}_{i=2}^{L}$  be functions satisfying the

following dynamics:

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}) = - \xi_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \Delta_ {1} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {1}) ],
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}) = - \xi_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \Delta_ {2} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}) ],
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}) = - \xi_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \Delta_ {i} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}) ], \qquad i = 3, \ldots , L - 2,
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}\right) = - \xi_ {L - 1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {w *} \left(t, Z, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}\right) \right],
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {L} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}) = - \xi_ {L} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \Delta_ {L} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}) ],
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}) = - \xi_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {b}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {i} ^ {b *} (t, Z) \right], \qquad i = 2, \dots , L - 2,
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}) = - \xi_ {L - 1} ^ {\mathbf {b}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {b *} (t, Z, u _ {L}) \right],
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} b _ {L} ^ {*} (t) = - \xi_ {L} ^ {\mathbf {b}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {L} ^ {b *} (t, Z) \right],
$$

$$
\forall u _ {i} \in \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i}\right) \text {f o r} i = 1, \dots , L,
$$

$$
\forall v _ {i} \in \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i}\right) \text {f o r} i = 2, \dots , L - 1,
$$

with the initialization  $w_1^*(0, u_1) = u_1$ ,  $w_2^*(0, \cdot, u_2, \cdot) = u_2$ ,  $w_i^*(0, u_i, \cdot, \cdot) = u_i$  for  $i = 3, \dots, L - 2$ ,  $w_{L-1}^*(0, u_{L-1}, \cdot, \cdot, \cdot) = u_{L-1}$ ,  $w_L^*(0, u_L, \cdot) = u_L$ ,  $b_i^*(0, v_i) = v_i$  for  $i = 2, \dots, L - 2$ ,  $b_{L-1}^*(0, \cdot, v_{L-1}) = v_{L-1}$  and  $b_L^*(0)$  a deterministic constant that  $\rho_{\mathbf{b}}^L(b_L^*(0)) = 1$  (i.e.  $b_L^*(0) = \mathfrak{p}_L(1)$  according to Eq. (8)). Here the quantities are defined by the following forward and backward recursions:

- Forward recursion:

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1}) = \phi_ {1} \left(w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}), x\right), \\ H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {2}) = \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}), \\ H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i}) = \int \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}), \\ i = 3, \dots , L - 2, \\ H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}) = \int \phi_ {L - 1} \left(w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}), b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), H _ {L - 2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {L - 2})\right) \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L - 1} (d u _ {L - 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {L - 2} (d v _ {L - 2}), \\ H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, x) = \int \phi_ {L} \left(w _ {L} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), b _ {L} ^ {*} (t), H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L} (d u _ {L}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {L - 1} (d v _ {L - 1}), \\ \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, x) = \phi_ {L + 1} \left(H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, x)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

- Backward recursion:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Delta_ {L} ^ {H *} (t, z) = \sigma_ {L} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, x)\right), \\ \Delta_ {L} ^ {w *} \left(t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right) = \sigma_ {L} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {L} ^ {H *} \left(t, z\right), w _ {L} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right), b _ {L} ^ {*} \left(t\right), H _ {L} ^ {*} \left(t, x\right), H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right)\right), \\ \Delta_ {L} ^ {b *} (t, z) = \int \sigma_ {L} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \left(\Delta_ {L} ^ {H *} (t, z), w _ {L} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), b _ {L} ^ {*} (t), H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1})\right) \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L} (d u _ {L}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {L - 1} (d v _ {L - 1}), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}) = \sigma_ {L - 1} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {L} ^ {H *} (t, z), w _ {L} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), b _ {L} ^ {*} (t), H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1})\right),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {w *} \left(t, z, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}\right) = \sigma_ {L - 1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \Big (\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H *} \left(t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right), w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}\right), b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right),
$$

$$
\left. H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), H _ {L - 2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {L - 2})\right),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {b *} \left(t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right) = \int \sigma_ {L - 1} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \Big (\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H *} \left(t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right), w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}\right), b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}\right),
$$

$$
\left. H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), H _ {L - 2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {L - 2})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L - 1} (d u _ {L - 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {L - 2} (d v _ {L - 2}),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {L - 2} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {L - 2}) = \int \sigma_ {L - 2} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \Big (\Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L - 1}, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 2}, v _ {L - 1}), b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}),
$$

$$
\left. H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {L}, v _ {L - 1}), H _ {L - 2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {L - 2})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L} (d u _ {L}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {L - 1} (d u _ {L - 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {L - 1} (d v _ {L - 1}),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {i} ^ {w *} (t, z, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}) = \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {i}), w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {i} ^ {b *} (t, z, v _ {i}) = \int \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \left(\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {i}), w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right)
$$

$$
\times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {i - 1} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {i - 1}) = \int \sigma_ {i - 1} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {i}), w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right)
$$

$$
\times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}),
$$

$$
\text {f o r} i = L - 2, \dots , 3,
$$

$$
\Delta_ {2} ^ {w *} (t, z, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}) = \sigma_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {2}), w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right),
$$

$$
\Delta_ {2} ^ {b *} (t, z, v _ {2}) = \int \sigma_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \left(\Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {2}), w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right)
$$

$$
\times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}),
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Delta_ {1} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {1}) = \int \sigma_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, v _ {2}), w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\Delta_ {1} ^ {w *} \left(t, z, u _ {1}\right) = \sigma_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {1} ^ {H *} \left(t, z, u _ {1}\right), w _ {1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {1}\right), x\right).
$$

In the case  $L = 3$  and  $L = 4$ , we define the dynamics of  $w_{i}^{*}$  and  $b_{i}^{*}$  similarly. In particular, for  $L = 4$ , one can simply disregard all above equations that are with invalid indices. For  $L = 3$ , we define:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}) = - \xi_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {1} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {1}) \right], \\ \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = - \xi_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \Delta_ {2} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) ], \\ \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = - \xi_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {3} ^ {w *} (t, Z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) \right], \\ \frac {\partial}{\partial t} b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = - \xi_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {b}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {2} ^ {b *} (t, Z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) \right], \\ \frac {\partial}{\partial t} b _ {3} ^ {*} (t) = - \xi_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {b}} (t) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {3} ^ {b *} (t, Z) \right], \\ \forall u _ {i} \in \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i}\right) \text {f o r} i = 1, 2, 3, \\ \forall v _ {i} \in \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i}\right) \text {f o r} i = 2, 3, \\ \end{array}
$$

in which the quantities are:

$$
H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1}) = \phi_ {1} \left(w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}), x\right),
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = \int \phi_ {2} (w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}), \\ H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x) = \int \phi_ {3} (w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {3} ^ {*} (t), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2})) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {3} (d u _ {3}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}), \\ \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, x) = \phi_ {4} \left(H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x)\right), \\ \Delta_ {3} ^ {H *} (t, z) = \sigma_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {H}} (y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x)), \\ \Delta_ {3} ^ {w *} (t, z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = \sigma_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {3} ^ {H *} (t, z), w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {3} ^ {*} (t), H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2})\right), \\ \Delta_ {3} ^ {b *} (t, z) = \int \sigma_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \left(\Delta_ {3} ^ {H *} (t, z), w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {3} ^ {*} (t), H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {3} (d u _ {3}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}), \\ \Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = \sigma_ {3} ^ {\mathbf {H}} (\Delta_ {3} ^ {H *} (t, z), w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {3} ^ {*} (t), H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, x), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2})), \\ \Delta_{2}^{w*}\left(t,z,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},v_{2}\right) = \sigma_{2}^{\mathbf{w}}\left(\Delta_{2}^{H*}\left(t,z,u_{3},v_{2}\right),w_{2}^{*}\left(t,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},v_{2}\right),b_{2}^{*}\left(t,u_{3},v_{2}\right),H_{2}^{*}\left(t,x,u_{3},v_{2}\right),H_{1}^{*}\left(t,x,u_{1}\right)\right), \\ \Delta_ {2} ^ {b *} (t, z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}) = \int \sigma_ {2} ^ {\mathbf {b}} \left(\Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}), \\ \Delta_ {1} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {1}) = \int \sigma_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {2} ^ {H *} (t, z, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {3}, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {3} (d u _ {3}), \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}), \\ \Delta_ {1} ^ {w *} \left(t, z, u _ {1}\right) = \sigma_ {1} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {1} ^ {H *} \left(t, z, u _ {1}\right), w _ {1} ^ {*} \left(t, u _ {1}\right), x\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally let  $W^{*}(t) = \{w_{1}^{*}(t,\cdot), w_{i}^{*}(t,\cdot), b_{i}^{*}(t,\cdot), i = 2,\dots,L\}$ . The existence and uniqueness of such dynamics follow similarly to the proof of Theorem 7.

Theorem 54 (Complete statement of Theorem 27). Given  $\left(\rho_{\mathbf{w}}^{1}, \ldots, \rho_{\mathbf{w}}^{L}, \rho_{\mathbf{b}}^{2}, \ldots, \rho_{\mathbf{b}}^{L}\right)$  and an integer  $M$ , construct the canonical neuronal ensemble  $\left(\Omega^{M}, P^{M}\right)$ , the random variables  $(C_{1}, \ldots, C_{L}) \sim P^{M} = \prod_{i=1}^{L} P_{i}^{M}$  and the canonical MF limit  $W^{M}$  as described in Section 5.1.1. Also construct the dynamics  $W^{*}$  described in Section 5.1.2.

For  $L\geq 5$  , define the following:

$$
w _ {1} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {1}) = w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (c _ {1})),
$$

$$
w _ {2} ^ {\infty} \left(t, c _ {1}, c _ {2}\right) = w _ {2} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {1} ^ {0} \left(c _ {1}\right), w _ {2} ^ {0} \left(c _ {1}, c _ {2}\right), b _ {2} ^ {0} \left(c _ {2}\right)\right),
$$

$$
w _ {i} ^ {\infty} \left(t, c _ {i - 1}, c _ {i}\right) = w _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(c _ {i - 1}, c _ {i}\right), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(c _ {i - 1}\right), b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(c _ {i}\right)\right), \qquad i = 3, \dots , L - 2,
$$

$$
w _ {L - 1} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {L - 2}, c _ {L - 1}) = w _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 2}, c _ {L - 1}), w _ {L} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 2} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 2}), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 1})),
$$

$$
w _ {L} ^ {\infty} \left(t, c _ {L - 1}, 1\right) = w _ {L} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {L} ^ {0} \left(c _ {L - 1}, 1\right), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} \left(c _ {L - 1}\right)\right),
$$

$$
b _ {i} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {i}) = b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (c _ {i})), \qquad i = 2, \dots , L - 2,
$$

$$
b _ {L - 1} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {L - 1}) = b _ {L - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {L} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (c _ {L - 1})\right),
$$

$$
b _ {L} ^ {\infty} (t, 1) = b _ {L} ^ {*} (t),
$$

$$
c _ {i} \in \Omega_ {i} = \Lambda \times \mathbb {N} _ {> 0}, \quad i = 1, \dots , L - 1.
$$

For  $L = 4$ , we define similarly by disregarding the equations with invalid indices. For  $L = 3$ , we define:

$$
w _ {1} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {1}) = w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (c _ {1})),
$$

$$
w _ {2} ^ {\infty} \left(t, c _ {1}, c _ {2}\right) = w _ {2} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {2} ^ {0} \left(c _ {1}, c _ {2}\right), w _ {3} ^ {0} \left(c _ {2}, 1\right), w _ {1} ^ {0} \left(c _ {1}\right), b _ {2} ^ {0} \left(c _ {2}\right)\right),
$$

$$
w _ {3} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {2}, 1) = w _ {3} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {3} ^ {0} (c _ {2}, 1), b _ {2} ^ {0} (c _ {2})\right),
$$

$$
b _ {2} ^ {\infty} (t, c _ {2}) = b _ {2} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {3} ^ {0} (c _ {2}, 1), b _ {2} ^ {0} (c _ {2})\right),
$$

$$
b _ {3} ^ {\infty} (t) = b _ {3} ^ {*} (t).
$$

We also let  $W^{\infty}(t) = \{w_{1}^{\infty}(t,\cdot), w_{i}^{\infty}(t,\cdot,\cdot), b_{i}^{\infty}(t,\cdot), i = 2,\dots,L\}$ . Let us consider:

$$
\left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} = \max \left(\max _ {1 \leq i \leq L} \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {M} - w _ {i} ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t}, \max _ {2 \leq i \leq L} \left\langle b _ {i} ^ {M} - b _ {i} ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t}\right),
$$

$$
\left\langle w _ {i} ^ {M} - w _ {i} ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {i} ^ {M} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) - w _ {i} ^ {\infty} (t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2},
$$

$$
\left\langle b _ {i} ^ {M} - b _ {i} ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| b _ {i} ^ {M} (t, C _ {i}) - b _ {i} ^ {\infty} (t, C _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2}, \qquad i = 2, \dots , L,
$$

$$
\left\langle w _ {1} ^ {M} - w _ {1} ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| w _ {1} ^ {M} (t, C _ {1}) - w _ {1} ^ {\infty} (t, C _ {1}) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

Then under Assumptions 1-3 and 5, for any  $T \geq 0$  and  $L \geq 2$ ,

$$
\sup  _ {t \leq T} \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M ^ {0 . 4 9 9}},
$$

for sufficiently large  $M = M(T,L)$ , where  $K_{T,L}$  is a constant that depends on  $T$  and  $L$ . Furthermore, for  $L \geq 4$  and  $2 \leq i \leq L - 2$ ,

$$
\sup  _ {t \leq T} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)\right) - H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M ^ {0 . 4 9 9}}.
$$

# D.2 Proof of Theorem 54

Proof of Theorem 54. Let us consider the case  $L \geq 5$ ; the case where  $L \leq 4$  is similarly proven. We use  $K_{T,L}$  to denote a generic constant that depends on  $T$  and  $L$  and may change from line to line.

Step 1. By following the argument of Lemma 8, one can show that the following quantities are bounded by  $K_{T,L}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| W^{\infty}\|_{T},\quad \mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\left|H^{*}_{1}\left(t,X,w^{0}_{1}(C_{1})\right)\right|^{50}\right],\quad \max_{2\leq i\leq L - 2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\left|H^{*}_{i}\left(t,X,b^{0}_{i}(C_{i})\right)\right|^{50}\right], \\ \mathbb {E} \left[ \sup _ {t \leq T} \underset {Z \sim \mathcal {P}} {\operatorname {e s s - s u p}} \left| H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, X, w _ {L} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1})) \right| ^ {5 0} \right], \quad \sup _ {t \leq T} \underset {Z \sim \mathcal {P}} {\operatorname {e s s - s u p}} \left| H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, X) \right|, \\ \mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\left|\Delta_{1}^{H*}\left(t,Z,w_{1}^{0}(C_{1})\right)\right|^{50}\right],\quad \max_{2\leq i\leq L - 2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\left|\Delta_{i}^{H*}\left(t,Z,b_{i}^{0}\left(C_{i}\right)\right)\right|^{50}\right], \\ \mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\bigl|\Delta^{H*}_{L - 1}\left(t,Z,w^{0}_{L}\left(C_{L - 1},1\right),b^{0}_{L - 1}(C_{L - 1})\right)\bigr|^{50}\right],\quad \sup_{t\leq T}\operatorname *{ess - sup}_{Z\sim \mathcal{P}}\bigl|\Delta^{H*}_{L}\left(t,Z\right)\bigr|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Likewise one can also show that for any  $B \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {T} ^ {w} \left(W ^ {\infty}\right) \geq K _ {T, L} B\right) \leq 2 K _ {T, L} e ^ {- K B ^ {2}},
$$

in which

$$
\max_{T}^{w}\left(W^{\infty}\right) = \max_{2\leq i\leq L}\sup_{t\leq T}\left|w_{i}^{\infty}\left(t,C_{i - 1},C_{i}\right)\right|.
$$

Step 2. Let us define

$$
D _ {1} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {1} (X, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, X, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1})) \right| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
D _ {i} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) - H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) \right| ^ {2} \right], \quad i = 2, \dots , L - 2,
$$

$$
D _ {L - 1} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {L - 1} (X, C _ {L - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - H _ {L - 1} ^ {*} (t, X, w _ {L} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1})) \right| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
D _ {L} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {L} (X, 1; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - H _ {L} ^ {*} (t, X) \right| ^ {2} \right].
$$

We claim that for  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
D _ {i} \left(t\right) \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M}, \quad i \in [ L ].
$$

Firstly it is immediate that

$$
H _ {1} \left(X, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) = \phi_ {1} \left(w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1})), X\right) = H _ {1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1})\right),
$$

and hence  $D_{1}(t) = 0$ . For  $i = 2$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {2} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1}), w _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {1}, C _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , H _ {1} (X, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t))\right) \right] \right. \right. \\ \left. - \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2}))  , b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2}))  , H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1})   \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \Bigg | ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Recalling that  $w_2^0 (C_1,C_2) = \mathfrak{q}_2(\theta_1,\theta_2)(\lambda_2)$ ,  $b_{2}^{0}(C_{2}) = \mathfrak{p}_{2}(\theta_{2})(\lambda_{2})$  and  $w_{1}^{0}(C_{1}) = \mathfrak{p}_{1}(\theta_{1})(\lambda_{1})$  from the construction of Section 5.1.1, we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {2}} \left[ \left. \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1}), w _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {1}, C _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , H _ {1} (x, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t))\right) \right] \right| ^ {2} \right] \right. \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \mathbb {E} _ {\theta_ {1}, \lambda_ {1}, \theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}, \lambda_ {1} ^ {\prime}, \theta_ {2}, \lambda_ {2}} \left[ \right.\left\langle \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, \mathfrak {p} _ {1} (\theta_ {1}) (\lambda_ {1}), \mathfrak {q} _ {2} (\theta_ {1}, \theta_ {2}) (\lambda_ {2}), \mathfrak {p} _ {2} (\theta_ {2}) (\lambda_ {2})\right), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, \mathfrak {p} _ {2} (\theta_ {2}) (\lambda_ {2})) , H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, \mathfrak {p} _ {1} (\theta_ {1}) (\lambda_ {1}))) \right., \\ \left. \left. \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} \left(t, \mathfrak {p} _ {1} \left(\theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \left(\lambda_ {1} ^ {\prime}\right), \mathfrak {q} _ {2} \left(\theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}, \theta_ {2}\right) \left(\lambda_ {2}\right), \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \left(\theta_ {2}\right) \left(\lambda_ {2}\right)\right), b _ {2} ^ {*} \left(t, \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \left(\theta_ {2}\right) \left(\lambda_ {2}\right)\right), H _ {1} ^ {*} \left(t, x, \mathfrak {p} _ {1} \left(\theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \left(\lambda_ {1} ^ {\prime}\right)\right)\right) \right\rangle \right] \\ \stackrel {(b)} {=} \mathbb {E} _ {\theta_ {1}, \theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}} \left[ \mathbb {I} (\theta_ {1} = \theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}) \int \left\langle \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right), \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1} ^ {\prime}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1} ^ {\prime})\right) \right\rangle \right. \\ \left. \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} \left(d u _ {1}\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} \left(d u _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} \left(d u _ {2}\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} \left(d v _ {2}\right) \right] \\ + \mathbb {E} _ {\theta_ {1}, \theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left(\theta_ {1} \neq \theta_ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1} ^ {\prime}, u _ {2} ^ {\prime}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1} ^ {\prime})\right) \right. \\ \left. \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2} ^ {\prime}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}) \right] \\ = \frac {1}{M} \int \left\langle \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right), \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1} ^ {\prime}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1} ^ {\prime})\right) \right\rangle \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}) \\ + \frac {M - 1}{M} \int \left| \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \right| ^ {2} \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where in step  $(a)$ ,  $(\theta_1', \lambda_1') \sim \mathrm{Unif}([M]) \times P_0$  is an independent copy of  $(\theta_1, \lambda_1)$  and is independent of  $(\theta_2, \lambda_2)$ , and step  $(b)$  is by the construction of  $\mathfrak{p}_1$ ,  $\mathfrak{p}_2$  and  $\mathfrak{q}_2$ . It is also easy to see that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {2}} \left[ \left\langle \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {1}} \left[ \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1}), w _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {1}, C _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) , H _ {1} (x, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t))\right) \right] , \right. \right. \\ \left. \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} \left(d u _ {1}\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} \left(d u _ {2}\right) \right\rangle \\ = \int \left| \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, x, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \right| ^ {2} \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, for  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {2} (t) \leq \frac {1}{M} \int \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \left\langle \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, X, u _ {1})\right), \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1} ^ {\prime}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, X, u _ {1} ^ {\prime})\right) \right\rangle \right| \right] \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}) \\ + \frac {1}{M} \int \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, X, u _ {1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}) \\ \leq \frac {K}{M} \int \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, v _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {*} (t, v _ {2}), H _ {1} ^ {*} (t, X, u _ {1})\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {1} (d u _ {1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} (d u _ {2}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {2} (d v _ {2}) \\ \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use Step 1 and Assumption 2 in the last step. For  $i \in \{3, \dots, L - 2\}$ , recall that  $w_i^0(C_{i-1}, C_i) = \mathfrak{q}_i(\theta_{i-1}, \theta_i)(\lambda_i)$ ,  $b_i^0(C_i) = \mathfrak{p}_i(\theta_i)(\lambda_i)$  and  $b_{i-1}^0(C_{i-1}) = \mathfrak{p}_{i-1}(\theta_{i-1})(\lambda_{i-1})$  from the construction of Section 5.1.1. Then similar to the argument for  $i = 2$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \left. \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right] \right| ^ {2} \right] \right. \\ = \frac {1}{M} \int \left\langle \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right), \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1} ^ {\prime}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1} ^ {\prime})\right) \right\rangle \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1} ^ {\prime}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}) \\ + \frac {M - 1}{M} \int \left| \int \phi_ {2} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \right| ^ {2} \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \left\langle \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right], \right. \right. \\ \left. \left. \int \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \right\rangle \right] \\ = \int \left| \int \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \right| ^ {2} \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}), \\ \end{array}
$$

which then gives, by Step 1 and Assumption 2:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right), b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right))\right) \right] \right. \\ \left. - H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} ] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i}} \Bigg [ \Bigg | \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. - \int \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \right| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {K}{M} \int \left| \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i}, v _ {i - 1}, v _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, x, v _ {i - 1})\right) \right| ^ {2} \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i} (d u _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i - 1} (d v _ {i - 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}) \\ \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, notice that again by Step 1 and Assumption 2:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Big | H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right. \\ \left. \left. - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right] \right| ^ {2} \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right), b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)), H _ {i - 1} \left(X, C _ {i - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right)\right) \right] \right. \\ \left. \left. - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \phi_ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right] \right| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i - 1}} \left[ \left(1 + \left| w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right), b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) \right| + \left| b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)) \right|\right) \right. \right. \\ \left. \times \left| H _ {i - 1} \left(X, C _ {i - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) - H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right)\right) \right| \right] ^ {2} \Bigg ] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(1 + \left| w _ {i} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| + \left| b _ {i} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right|\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ \times \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i - 1} (X, C _ {i - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1})) \right| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq K _ {T, L} D _ {i - 1} (t). \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence,

$$
D _ {i} (t) \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M} + K _ {T, L} D _ {i - 1} (t).
$$

This proves the claim for  $i \leq L - 2$ . The other claims are similar.

Step 3. Let us define:

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {1} ^ {H} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (Z, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \\ D _ {i} ^ {H} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {H} (Z, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \qquad i = 2, \dots , L - 2, \\ D _ {L - 1} ^ {H} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H} (Z, C _ {L - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, w _ {L} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \\ D _ {L} ^ {H} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L} ^ {H} (Z, 1; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {L} ^ {H *} (t, Z) \right| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We claim that for  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
D _ {i} ^ {H} (t) \leq K _ {T, L} \frac {\log^ {1 / 2} M}{M}, \qquad i \in [ L ].
$$

The derivation is similar to Step 2; let us give a sketch and highlight the difference. The last claim for  $i = L$  is immediate from Assumption 3 and Step 2. Let us consider the claim for  $2 \leq i \leq L - 3$ ; the rest

of the claims are similar. We have:

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \Bigg | \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) , w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}) , b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}) , b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})\right) \right. \right.,
$$

$$
\left. \left. b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right)\right) \right] - \Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})\right) \left| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
= \mathbb {E} \left[ \Bigg | \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) , w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})\right), \right. \right.
$$

$$
\left. b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right)\right) \Bigg ]
$$

$$
- \int \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, v _ {i + 1}), w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i + 1}, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), v _ {i + 1}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i + 1}), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, X, v _ {i + 1}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}))\right)
$$

$$
\left. \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i + 1} (d u _ {i + 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i + 1} (d v _ {i + 1}) \right| ^ {2}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \frac {K}{M} \int \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H _ {*}} (t, Z, v _ {i + 1}), w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i + 1}, v _ {i}, v _ {i + 1}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i + 1}), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, X, v _ {i + 1}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, v _ {i})\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i + 1} (d u _ {i + 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i + 1} (d v _ {i + 1}) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {K}{M} \int \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, v _ {i + 1}) \right| ^ {2}\right) \left(1 + \left| w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {i + 1}, v _ {i}, v _ {i + 1}) \right| ^ {2} + \left| b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, v _ {i + 1}) \right| ^ {2}\right) \right] \\ \times \rho_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {i + 1} (d u _ {i + 1}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i} (d v _ {i}) \rho_ {\mathbf {b}} ^ {i + 1} (d v _ {i + 1}) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\stackrel {(c)} {\leq} \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M},
$$

where  $(a)$  is similar to Step 2 in which we use the fact  $w_{i + 1}^{0}(C_{i},C_{i + 1}) = \mathfrak{q}_{i + 1}(\theta_{i},\theta_{i + 1})(\lambda_{i + 1})$ ,  $b_{i}^{0}(C_{i}) = \mathfrak{p}_{i}(\theta_{i})(\lambda_{i})$  and  $b_{i + 1}^{0}(C_{i + 1}) = \mathfrak{p}_{i + 1}(\theta_{i + 1})(\lambda_{i + 1})$  from the construction of Section 5.1.1,  $(b)$  is by Assumption 3, and  $(c)$  is follows from Step 1. We also note:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \bigg [ \Big | \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \Big [ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \Big (\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) \:, w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}) \:, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}) \:, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1}) \: \Big) \:, \\ \left. b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right)\right)\left. \right] - \Delta_ {i} ^ {H} \left(Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}); W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \Bigg | ^ {2} \\ = \mathbb {E} \left[ \Bigg | \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \left(\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) , w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})\right), \right. \right. \\ \left. b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right)\right) \Bigg ] \\ - \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \Big [ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {H}} \Big (\Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H} (Z, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)), w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1}) \Big), \\ \left. \left. b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right), H _ {i + 1} \left(X, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right), H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right)\right) \right] \Bigg | ^ {2} \Bigg ] \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} K \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \left(1 + \left| w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})\right) \right| + \left| b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) \right|\right) \right. \\ \left. \times \left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right) - \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H} \left(Z, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right| \right] ^ {2} \Bigg ] \\ + K \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \left(1 + \left| w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})\right) \right| + \left| b _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) \right|\right) \right. \\ \times \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right) \right| + \left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right) - \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H} \left(Z, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right|\right) \\ \times \left(\left| H _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) - H _ {i + 1} (X, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right| \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \left. + \left| H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) - H _ {i} (X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right|\right) \right] ^ {2} \Bigg ] \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} K _ {T, L} \left(D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t) + D _ {i + 1} (t) + D _ {i} (t)\right) + K Q _ {i} (t), \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(a)$  follows from Assumption 3,  $(b)$  follows from Step 1, and we define

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {i} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {C _ {i + 1}} \left[ \left| w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} (t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) \right| \right. \right. \\ \times \left(\left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) \right| + \left| \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H *} (t, Z, b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i + 1})) - \Delta_ {i + 1} ^ {H} (Z, C _ {i + 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right|\right) \\ \left. \left. \times \left| H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) - H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right|\right) \right] ^ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

The bounding of  $Q_{i}(t)$  requires some more care. In particular, for  $B > 0$ , define

$$
E = \left\{\left| w _ {i + 1} ^ {*} \left(t, w _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}, C _ {i + 1}\right), b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right), b _ {i + 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i + 1}\right)\right) \right| \geq B \right\}.
$$

Upon decomposing the inner expectation of  $Q_{i}(t)$  into the sum of  $\mathbb{I}(E)$  and  $\mathbb{I}(\neg E)$ , together with Step 1, via an appropriate use of Cauchy-Schwarz's inequality, it is easy to see that

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {i} (t) \leq K _ {T, L} B \left(D _ {i} (t) + D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t)\right) + K _ {T, L} \left(1 + D _ {i} (t) + D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t)\right) \mathbb {P} (E) ^ {1 / 8} \\ \leq K _ {T, L} (1 + B) \left(D _ {i} (t) + D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t)\right) + K _ {T, L} e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which holds for any  $B > 0$ . Combining these bounds together and Step 2, we obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {i} ^ {H} (t) \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M} + K _ {T, L} (1 + B) \left(D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t) + D _ {i + 1} (t) + D _ {i} (t)\right) + K _ {T, L} e ^ {- K B ^ {2}} \\ \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M} + K _ {T, L} (1 + B) \left(D _ {i + 1} ^ {H} (t) + \frac {1}{M}\right) + K _ {T, L} e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then choosing  $B = c\sqrt{\log M}$  for an appropriate constant  $c$  leads to the desired conclusion.

Step 4. Let us define:

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {1} ^ {w} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {1} ^ {w *} (t, Z, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \\ D _ {2} ^ {w} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {1}, C _ {2}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {2} ^ {w *} (t, Z, w _ {1} ^ {0} (C _ {1}), w _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {1}, C _ {2}), b _ {2} ^ {0} (C _ {2})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
D _ {i} ^ {w} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {w *} (t, Z, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \quad i = 3,..., L - 2
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {L - 1} ^ {w} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {w} \left(Z, C _ {L - 2}, C _ {L - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right. \right. \\ \left. - \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {w *} \left(t, Z, w _ {L - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {L - 2}, C _ {L - 1}\right), w _ {L} ^ {0} \left(C _ {L - 1}, 1\right), b _ {L - 2} ^ {0} \left(C _ {L - 2}\right), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {L - 1}\right)\right) \right] \Bigg ], \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
D _ {L} ^ {w} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {L - 1}, 1; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {L} ^ {w *} (t, Z, w _ {L} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1})) \right| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
D _ {i} ^ {b} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {b} (Z, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {b *} (t, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \quad i = 2, \dots , L - 2,
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {L - 1} ^ {b} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {b} (Z, C _ {L - 1}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {L - 1} ^ {b *} (t, Z, w _ {L} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1}, 1), b _ {L - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {L - 1})) \right| ^ {2} \right], \\ D _ {L} ^ {b} (t) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {L} ^ {b} (Z, 1; W ^ {\infty} (t)) - \Delta_ {L} ^ {b *} (t, Z) \right| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We claim that for any  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
\max  _ {1 \leq i \leq L} D _ {i} ^ {w} (t) \leq K _ {T, L} \frac {\log^ {1 / 2} M}{M}, \quad \max  _ {2 \leq i \leq L} D _ {i} ^ {b} (t) \leq K _ {T, L} \frac {\log^ {1 / 2} M}{M}.
$$

Indeed by Assumption 3, for  $3 \leq i \leq L - 2$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {i} ^ {w} (t) \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ 1 + \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) \right| ^ {2} + \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} \left(t, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {H} \left(Z, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] D _ {i - 1} (t) \\ + K \left(D _ {i} ^ {H} (t) + D _ {i} (t)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

The claim for  $D_i^w$  then follows from Steps 1, 2 and 3. The rest are similar.

Step 5. With the same argument as Lemma 49, given Step 1, one gets that for  $2 \leq i \leq L$ , any  $t \leq T$  and any  $B \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right] \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq K _ {T, L} \left((1 + B) \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right).
$$

As such, by Step 4,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {w *} (t, Z, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}), b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq \left| D _ {i} ^ {w} (t) \right| ^ {1 / 2} + \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)) - \Delta_ {i} ^ {w} (Z, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq K _ {T, L} \left(\frac {\log^ {1 / 4} M}{M ^ {1 / 2}} + (1 + B) \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

One can obtain similar results for  $\Delta_1^w$  and  $\Delta_i^b$ . Hence, we obtain that for all  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
\left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} \leq K _ {T, L} \int_ {0} ^ {t} \left(\frac {\log^ {1 / 4} M}{M ^ {1 / 2}} + (1 + B) \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right) d s.
$$

Since  $\left\langle W^M -W^\infty \right\rangle_0 = 0$ , Gronwall's inequality implies that

$$
\sup _ {t \leq T} \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t} \leq K _ {T, L} \inf _ {B > 0} \left[ \left(\frac {\log^ {1 / 4} M}{M ^ {1 / 2}} + e ^ {- K B ^ {2}}\right) e ^ {K _ {T, L} (1 + B)} \right] \leq K _ {T, L} \frac {1}{M ^ {0 . 4 9 9}},
$$

for sufficiently large  $M$ .

Furthermore, with the same argument as Lemma 48, given Step 1, one gets that for  $2 \leq i \leq L - 2$  and any  $t \leq T$ ,

$$
