\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i} (X, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)) - H _ {i} (X, C _ {i}; W ^ {\infty} (t)) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq K _ {T, L} \left\langle W ^ {M} - W ^ {\infty} \right\rangle_ {t}.
$$

As such, together with Step 2, we get

$$
\sup  _ {t \leq T} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i} (X, C _ {i}; W ^ {M} (t)) - H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq K _ {T, L} \frac {1}{M ^ {0 . 4 9 9}},
$$

for sufficiently large  $M$ .

![](images/332127a447bf911b86fd62abb0c0d5898310c76f67551ab6b73be295631670f6.jpg)

# D.3 Proof of Proposition 26

Proof of Proposition 26. It is easy to see that under the canonical neuronal ensemble  $\left(\Omega^{M}, P^{M}\right)$ , the functions  $\{w_{i}^{0}\}_{i=1}^{L}$  and  $\{b_{i}^{0}\}_{i=2}^{L}$  satisfy the i.i.d. initialization law, according to Eq. (4)-(8). To derive the  $\bar{\eta}$ -independence property, recall from the construction that for  $i \leq L-1$ ,  $C_{i}(j_{i}) = (\lambda_{i}(j_{i}), \theta_{i}(j_{i}))$  and  $\{C_{i}(j_{i})\}_{j_{i} \in [n_{i}]}$  are sampled from  $(P_{0} \times \mathrm{Unif}([M]))^{n_{i}}$  conditional on that  $\{\theta_{i}(j_{i})\}_{j_{i} \in [n_{i}]}$  are all distinct. Notice then for  $i \leq L-1$  and any  $j \in [n_{i}]$ :

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ f (C _ {i} (j)) \mid \{C _ {i} (h), h \neq j \} \right] = \frac {1}{M - n _ {i} + 1} \sum_ {\theta \notin \{\theta_ {i} (h) \colon h \neq j \}} \mathbb {E} \left[ f (C _ {i} (j)) \mid \theta_ {i} (h) = \theta \right].
$$

Thus, for 1-bounded function  $f$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \right.\left| \mathbb {E} \left[ f \left(C _ {i} (j)\right) \mid \{C _ {i} (h), h \neq j \} \right] - \mathbb {E} \left[ f \left(C _ {i} (j)\right)\right]\right| \\ \leq \frac{1}{M}\sum_{\theta \in \{\theta_{i}(h):h\neq j\}}|\mathbb{E}\left[f(C_{i}(j))\mid \theta_{i}(h) = \theta \right]| + \frac{n_{i} - 1}{M(M - n_{i} + 1)}\sum_{\theta \notin \{\theta_{i}(h):h\neq j\}}|\mathbb{E}\left[f(C_{i}(j))\mid \theta_{i}(h) = \theta \right]| \\ \leq 2 \frac {n _ {i} - 1}{M}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The claim is trivial for  $i = L$ .

![](images/cf3a4c3b1e2e707eece9935f5d499f522672de600f82ae97929b9141fd697193.jpg)

# D.4 Proofs of Corollaries 29 and 30

Proof of Corollary 29. By Proposition 26 and Corollary 17 (in particular, one of the intermediate steps in its proof), we have that for sufficiently large  $M$ , with probability at least  $1 - 3\delta - KLn_{\mathrm{max}} \exp \left(-Kn_{\mathrm{min}}^{c_2}\right)$

$$
\left(\frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| \mathbf {H} _ {i} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor , X, j _ {i}\right) - H _ {i} \left(X, C _ {i} \left(j _ {i}\right); W ^ {M} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon\right)\right) \right| ^ {2} \right]\right) ^ {1 / 2} = \tilde {\cal O} \left(n _ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {- c _ {1}} + \epsilon^ {c _ {1}}\right),
$$

where we recall that  $\{C_i(j_i)\}_{j_i\in [n_i]}$  are sampled according to the sampling rule  $\overline{P}_{\mathbf{n}}^{M}$  as described in Section 5.1.1. On the other hand, since  $\mathrm{Law}(C_i(j_i)) = P_i^M$ , by Theorem 27,

$$
\mathbb {E} \bigg [ \frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| H _ {i} (X, C _ {i} (j _ {i}); W ^ {M} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon)) - H _ {i} ^ {*} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, B _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \bigg ] ^ {1 / 2} \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{M ^ {0 . 4 9 9}},
$$

which yields for any  $\gamma > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \bigg (\frac {1}{n _ {i}} \sum_ {j _ {i} = 1} ^ {n _ {i}} \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| H _ {i} (X, C _ {i} (j _ {i}); W ^ {M} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon)) - H _ {i} ^ {*} (\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, B _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] \geq \gamma \bigg) \leq \frac {K _ {T , L}}{\gamma M ^ {0 . 9}}.
$$

Finally by following the argument in the proof of Corollary 17, we have:

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(\lfloor t / \epsilon \rfloor \epsilon , X, B _ {i}\right) - H _ {i} ^ {*} (t, X, B _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \leq K _ {T, L} \epsilon .
$$

The proof concludes by combining this with the previous two probability bounds and taking  $M \to \infty$  then  $\gamma \to 0$ .

Proof of Corollary 30. For  $3 \leq i \leq L - 2$ , since  $b_i^0(C_i) = B_i$  a constant,

$$
\begin{array}{l} w _ {i} ^ {\infty} \left(t, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) - w _ {i} ^ {\infty} \left(0, C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}\right) \\ = - \int_ {0} ^ {t} \xi_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (s) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \Big [ \sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \Big (\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (s, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i})) , w _ {i} ^ {*} (s, w _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}, C _ {i}) , b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}), b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}) \Big), \\ \left. b _ {i} ^ {*} \left(s, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right), H _ {i} ^ {*} \left(s, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i}\right)\right), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} \left(s, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} \left(C _ {i - 1}\right)\right)\right)\left. \right] d s \\ = - \int_ {0} ^ {t} \xi_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (s) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (s, Z, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}))  , b _ {i} ^ {*} (s, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}))  , H _ {i} ^ {*} (s, X, b _ {i} ^ {0} (C _ {i}))  , H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (s, X, b _ {i - 1} ^ {0} (C _ {i - 1}))\right) \right] d s \\ = - \int_ {0} ^ {t} \xi_ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} (s) \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {i} ^ {\mathbf {w}} \left(\Delta_ {i} ^ {H *} (s, Z, B _ {i}), b _ {i} ^ {*} (s, B _ {i}), H _ {i} ^ {*} (s, X, B _ {i}), H _ {i - 1} ^ {*} (s, X, B _ {i - 1})\right) \right] d s, \\ \end{array}
$$

which is independent of  $C_{i-1}$  and  $C_i$ . The desired claim readily follows.

# E Remaining proofs for Section 6

# E.1 Proof of Theorem 31

First we show that if  $w_{1}(0,C_{1})$  has full support, then so is  $w_{1}(t,C_{1})$  at any time  $t$ . Note that the following result holds beyond the setting of Theorem 31.

Lemma 55. Consider the MF ODEs (as described in Section 2.2) with  $L = 2$  and  $\mathbb{W}_1 = \mathbb{R}^d$  (for some positive integer  $d$ ), under Assumptions 1-3 and 5. Let us disregard the bias of the second layer by considering  $\xi_2^{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot) = 0$  and  $b_{2}(0,\cdot) = 0$ . Suppose that the support of Law  $(w_{1}(0,C_{1}),w_{2}(0,C_{1},1))$  contains the graph of a continuous function  $F: \mathbb{W}_1 \to \mathbb{W}_2$  such that  $|F(u)| \leq K$  for all  $u \in \mathbb{W}_1$ . Then for all finite time  $t$ , the support of Law  $(w_{1}(t,C_{1}))$  is  $\mathbb{W}_1$ .

Proof. Since the support of  $\operatorname{Law}(w_1(0, C_1), w_2(0, C_1, 1))$  contains the graph of  $F: \mathbb{W}_1 \to \mathbb{W}_2$ , we can choose the neuronal embedding so that there is a choice  $C_1(u)$  for each  $u \in \mathbb{W}_1$  such that  $w_1(0, C_1(u)) = u$  and  $w_2(0, C_1(u), 1) = F(u)$ , and furthermore for any neighborhood  $U$  of  $(u, F(u))$ ,  $(w_1(0, C_1), w_2(0, C_1, 1))$  lies in  $U$  with positive probability. For an arbitrary  $T \geq 0$ , let us define  $M: [0, T] \times \mathbb{W}_1 \to \mathbb{W}_1$  by  $M(t, u) = w_1(t, C_1(u))$ .

We show that  $M$  is continuous. In the following, we define  $K_{t}$  to be a generic constant that changes with  $t$  and is finite with finite  $t$ . We first have from Assumption 3 that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {H} (t, z, 1) \right| \leq K, \\ \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1}, 1) \right| \leq K \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {H} (t, z, 1) \right|\right) \leq K, \\ \end{array}
$$

which implies, by Assumption 1,

$$
\left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) \right| \leq \left| w _ {2} (0, c _ {1}, 1) \right| + K _ {t}.
$$

In particular, for any  $u \in \mathbb{W}_1$ ,

$$
\left| w _ {2} (t, C _ {1} (u), 1) \right| \leq F (u) + K _ {t} \leq K _ {t}.
$$

We then have from Assumptions 2-3 that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1}) - H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right| \leq K \left| w _ {1} (t, c _ {1}) - w _ {1} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right|, \\ \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1}, 1) - \Delta_ {2} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right| \leq K \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {H} (t, z, 1) \right|\right) \left| H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1}) - H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right| \\ + K \left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) - w _ {2} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right| \\ \leq K \left(\left| w _ {2} \left(t, c _ {1}, 1\right) - w _ {2} \left(t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1\right) \right| + \left| w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1}\right) - w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right|\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1}) - \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right| \leq K \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {H} (t, z, 1) \right|\right) \left(\left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) - w _ {2} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right| \right. \\ \left. + \left(1 + \left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) \right| + \left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right|\right) \left| H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1}) - H _ {1} (t, x, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right|\right) \\ \leq K \left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) - w _ {2} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right| \\ + K _ {t} \left(1 + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, c _ {1}, 1\right) \right| + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1\right) \right|\right) \left| w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1}\right) - w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right|, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1}) - \Delta_ {1} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right| \leq K \left(\left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1}) - \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right| + \left| w _ {1} (t, c _ {1}) - w _ {1} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}) \right|\right) \\ \leq K \left| w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) - w _ {2} (t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \right| \\ + K _ {t} \left(1 + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, c _ {1}, 1\right) \right| + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1\right) \right|\right) \left| w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1}\right) - w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Defining

$$
R (t) = \left| w _ {2} \left(t, C _ {1} (u), 1\right) - w _ {2} \left(t, C _ {1} \left(u ^ {\prime}\right), 1\right) \right| ^ {2} + \left| w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} (u)\right) - w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} \left(u ^ {\prime}\right)\right) \right| ^ {2}
$$

for some  $u, u' \in \mathbb{W}_1$ , we then have for any  $t \leq T$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {d}{d t} R (t) \leq K _ {T} \left(1 + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, C _ {1} (u), 1\right) \right| + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, C _ {1} \left(u ^ {\prime}\right), 1\right) \right|\right) ^ {2} R (t) \\ = K _ {T} \left(1 + | F (u) | + | F (u ^ {\prime}) |\right) ^ {2} R (t) \\ \leq K _ {T} R (t), \\ \end{array}
$$

which implies that  $R(t) \leq R(0)\exp(K_Tt)$ . In addition, by Assumption 3,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1}) \right| \leq K \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {2} ^ {H} (t, z, 1) \right|\right) (1 + | w _ {2} (t, c _ {1}, 1) |) \\ \leq K \left| w _ {2} (0, c _ {1}, 1) \right| + K _ {t}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {w} (t, z, c _ {1}) \right| \leq K \left(1 + \left| \Delta_ {1} ^ {H} (t, z, c _ {1}) \right|\right) \\ \leq K \left| w _ {2} (0, c _ {1}, 1) \right| + K _ {t}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which leads to

$$
\left| w _ {1} \left(t, c _ {1}\right) - w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, c _ {1}\right) \right| \leq K _ {t \vee t ^ {\prime}} \left(1 + \left| w _ {2} \left(0, c _ {1}, 1\right) \right|\right) \left| t - t ^ {\prime} \right|.
$$

Since  $R(0) = |F(u) - F(u')|^2 + |u - u'|^2 \to 0$  as  $u \to u'$ , we deduce that, for  $t, t' \leq T$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} (u)\right) - w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1} \left(u ^ {\prime}\right)\right) \right| \leq \left| w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} (u)\right) - w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1} (u)\right) \right| \\ + \left| w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1} (u)\right) - w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1} \left(u ^ {\prime}\right)\right) \right| \\ \leq K _ {T} \left(1 + | F (u) |\right) \left| t - t ^ {\prime} \right| + \sqrt {R (0)} \exp \left(K _ {T} T\right) \\ \rightarrow 0 \\ \end{array}
$$

as  $(u,t)\to (u^{\prime},t^{\prime})$ . This shows that  $M(t,u) = w_{1}(t,C_{1}(u))$  is continuous.

Recall that  $\mathbb{W}_1 = \mathbb{R}^d$ , and consider the sphere  $\mathbb{S}^d$  which is a compactification of  $\mathbb{W}_1$ . We extend  $M:[0,T]\times \mathbb{S}^d\to \mathbb{S}^d$  fixing the point at infinity, which remains a continuous map since

$$
| M (t, u) - u | = | M (t, u) - M (0, u) | = | w _ {1} (t, C _ {1} (u)) - w _ {1} (0, C _ {1} (u)) | \leq K _ {T} (1 + | F (u) |) t \leq K _ {T} t.
$$

Let  $M_t: \mathbb{W}_1 \to \mathbb{W}_1$  be defined by  $M_t(u) = M(t, u)$ . Observe that if  $M_t$  is surjective for all  $t$ , then the support of  $\operatorname{Law}(w_1(t, C_1))$  is  $\mathbb{W}_1$ , since for a neighborhood  $B$  of  $M(t, u) = w_1(t, C_1(u))$ ,  $\mathbb{P}(w_1(t, C_1) \in B) = \mathbb{P}(w_1(0, C_1) \in M_t^{-1}(B)) > 0$ . It is indeed true that  $M_t$  is surjective for all  $t$  for the following reason. If  $M_t$  fails to be surjective for some  $t$ , then for some  $p \in \mathbb{S}^d$ ,  $M_t: \mathbb{S}^d \to \mathbb{S}^d \setminus \{p\} \to \mathbb{S}^d$  is homotopic to the constant map, but  $M$  then gives a homotopy from the identity map  $M_0$  on the sphere to a constant map, which is a contradiction as the sphere  $\mathbb{S}^d$  is not contractible. This finishes the proof of the claim.

We are ready to prove Theorem 31. We recall the setting of Theorem 31, and in particular, the neural network (9).

Proof of Theorem 31. It is easy to check that Assumptions 1-3 hold. Therefore, by Theorem 7, the solution to the MF ODEs exists uniquely, and by Lemma 55, the support of  $\operatorname{Law}(w_1(t,C_1))$  is  $\mathbb{R}^d$  at all  $t$ . We recall from the convergence assumption the limits  $\bar{w}_1$  and  $\bar{w}_2$ , and we shall first prove  $(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)$  is a global minimizer of  $\mathcal{L}$  in Case 1 and  $\mathcal{L}(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2) = 0$  in Case 2.

By the convergence assumption, we have that for any  $\epsilon > 0$ , there exists  $T(\epsilon)$  such that for all  $t \geq T(\epsilon)$  and  $P$ -almost every  $c_{1}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \epsilon \geq \left| \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (H _ {2} (t, X, 1)) \varphi_ {1} (\langle w _ {1} (t, c _ {1}), X \rangle) \right] \right| \\ = \Big | \left\langle \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} \left(Y, \hat {y} (t, X)\right) \right| X = x \right] \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} (t, x, 1)\right), \varphi_ {1} \left(\left\langle w _ {1} (t, c _ {1}), x \right\rangle\right) \Big \rangle_ {L ^ {2} (\mathcal {P} _ {X})} \Big |. \\ \end{array}
$$

Let  $\mathcal{H}(f_1, f_2, x) = \mathbb{E}_Z[\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y, \hat{y}(X; f_1, f_2))|X = x] \varphi_2'(H_2(x; f_1, f_2))$ . Since  $\operatorname{Law}(w_1(t, C_1))$  has full support, we obtain that for  $u$  in a dense subset of  $\mathbb{R}^d$ ,

$$
\left| \langle \mathcal {H} (w _ {1} (t, \cdot), w _ {2} (t, \cdot , 1), x), \varphi_ {1} (\langle u, x \rangle) \rangle_ {L ^ {2} (\mathcal {P} _ {X})} \right| \leq \epsilon .
$$

Since  $\varphi_1'$  is bounded and  $|X| \leq K$ ,  $\mathbb{E}_X\left[|\varphi_1(\langle u', X \rangle) - \varphi_1(\langle u, X \rangle)|^2\right] \to 0$  as  $u' \to u$ . Hence,

$$
\left| \langle \mathcal {H} (w _ {1} (t, \cdot), w _ {2} (t, \cdot , 1), x), \varphi_ {1} (\langle u, x \rangle) \rangle_ {L ^ {2} (\mathcal {P} _ {X})} \right| \leq \epsilon ,
$$

for all  $u \in \mathbb{R}^d$ . We claim that  $\mathcal{H}(w_1(t,\cdot), w_2(t,\cdot,1), X) \to \mathcal{H}(\bar{w}_1, \bar{w}_2, X)$  in  $L^1(\mathcal{P}_X)$  as  $t \to \infty$ . Assuming this claim, since  $\varphi_1$  is bounded, we have for every  $u \in \mathbb{R}^d$ ,

$$
\langle \mathcal {H} (\bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}, x), \varphi_ {1} (u, x) \rangle_ {L ^ {2} (\mathcal {P} _ {X})} = 0.
$$

Since  $\{\varphi_1\left(\langle u,\cdot \rangle\right):u\in \mathbb{R}^d\}$  has dense span in  $L^2 (\mathcal{P}_X)$

$$
\mathcal {H} \left(\bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}, x\right) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} \left(Y, \hat {y} \left(X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) | X = x \right] \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} \left(x; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) = 0,
$$

for  $\mathcal{P}_X$ -almost every  $x$ .

In Case 1,  $\varphi_2^\prime$  is non-zero, we get  $\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_2\mathcal{L}\left(Y,\hat{y} (X;\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)\right)|X = x\right] = 0$  for  $\mathcal{P}_X$ -almost every  $x$ . For  $\mathcal{L}$  convex in the second variable, for any measurable function  $\tilde{y} (x)$ ,

$$
\mathcal {L} \left(y, \tilde {y} (x)\right) - \mathcal {L} \left(y, \hat {y} \left(x; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) \geq \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} \left(y, \hat {y} \left(x; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) \left(\tilde {y} (x) - \hat {y} \left(x; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right).
$$

Taking expectation, we get  $\mathbb{E}_Z[\mathcal{L}(Y,\tilde{y} (X))]\geq \mathcal{L}(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)$ , i.e.  $(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)$  is a global minimizer of  $\mathcal{L}$ .

In Case 2, since  $y$  is a function of  $x$  and  $\varphi_2^\prime$  is non-zero, we obtain  $\partial_2\mathcal{L}(y,\hat{y} (x;\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)) = 0$  and hence  $\mathcal{L}(y,\hat{y} (x;\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)) = 0$  for  $\mathcal{P}_X$ -almost every  $x$ . That is,  $\mathcal{L}(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2) = 0$ .

We now prove the claim. Using the assumptions and recalling the coupling  $\pi_t$  in Assumption 6.4:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathcal {H} \left(w _ {1} (t, \cdot), w _ {2} (t, \cdot , 1), X\right) - \mathcal {H} \left(\bar {w} _ {1} (t, \cdot), \bar {w} _ {2} (t, \cdot , 1), X\right) \right| \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; w _ {1}, w _ {2})) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (H _ {2} (X; w _ {1}, w _ {2})) - \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2})) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (H _ {2} (X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2})) \right| \right] \\ \leq K \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} \left(X; w _ {1}, w _ {2}\right)\right) - \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} \left(X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) \right| + \left| \varphi_ {2} \left(H _ {2} \left(X; w _ {1}, w _ {2}\right)\right) - \varphi_ {2} \left(H _ {2} \left(X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}\right)\right) \right| \right]\right) \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| H _ {2} (X; w _ {1}, w _ {2}) - H _ {2} (X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}) \right| \right] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {\pi_ {t}} \left[ | \bar {w} _ {2} \left(C _ {1}\right) - w _ {2} \left(t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1\right) | + | \bar {w} _ {2} \left(C _ {1}\right) | \mid \varphi_ {1} \left(\langle w _ {1} \left(t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right), x \rangle\right) - \varphi_ {1} \left(\langle \bar {w} _ {1} \left(C _ {1}\right), x \rangle\right) | \right] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {\pi_ {t}} \left[ | \bar {w} _ {2} (C _ {1}) - w _ {2} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) | + | \bar {w} _ {2} (C _ {1}) | | w _ {1} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}) - \bar {w} _ {1} (C _ {1}) | \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

which converges to 0 by assumption. This proves the claim.

Finally to connect  $\mathcal{L}(\bar{w}_1,\bar{w}_2)$  with  $\mathcal{L}(W(t))$  in the limit  $t\to \infty$ , we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \left| \mathcal {L} (W (t)) - \mathcal {L} (\bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}) \right| = \left| \mathbb {E} _ {Z} [ \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; W (t))) - \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2})) ] \right| \right. \\ \leq K \left| \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \hat {y} (X; W (t)) - \hat {y} (X; \bar {w} _ {1}, \bar {w} _ {2}) \right] \right| \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {\pi_ {t}} \left[ | \bar {w} _ {2} (C _ {1}) | | w _ {1} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}) - \bar {w} _ {1} (C _ {1}) | + | w _ {2} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) - \bar {w} _ {2} (C _ {1}) | \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

which again converges to 0 by assumption. This completes the proof.

# E.2 Proof of Proposition 36

Proof of Proposition 36. We recall

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}) = - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, X)) w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}) \right. \\ \left. \times \varphi_ {3} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {3} ^ {*} (t, X)\right) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} ^ {*} (t, X, u _ {3})\right) \varphi_ {1} \left(\langle w _ {1} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}), X \rangle\right) \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $u_{1}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$ ,  $u_{2}\in \operatorname {supp}\left(\rho^{2}\right)$ ,  $u_{3}\in \operatorname {supp}\left(\rho^{3}\right)$ . By the regularity assumption,

$$
\left| \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, u _ {3}) \right| \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ | \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, X)) | \right] | w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, u _ {3}) |.
$$

Note that the right-hand side is independent of  $u_{1}$  and  $u_{2}$ . Since  $\int |w_3^* (t,u_3') - \bar{w}_3(u_3)|d\pi_t^3 (u_3,u_3')\to 0$  as  $t\to \infty$  for a coupling  $\pi_t^3$  of  $\rho_{3}$  and itself, we have for some finite  $t_0\leq K$

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ | \bar {w} _ {3} (U _ {3}) | \right] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ | w _ {3} ^ {*} \left(t _ {0}, U _ {3}\right) | \right] + K \leq K,
$$

where the last step is by an argument similar to the proof of Lemma 8 and the initialization assumption. As such, for all  $t$  sufficiently large, we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup _ {u _ {1} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d}, u _ {2} \in \operatorname {s u p p} (\rho^ {2})} \mathbb {E} _ {U _ {3} \sim \rho^ {3}} \left[ \left| \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} ^ {*} (t, u _ {1}, u _ {2}, U _ {3}) \right| \right] \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ | \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, X)) | \right] \mathbb {E} \left[ | w _ {3} ^ {*} (t, U _ {3}) | \right] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} \left(Y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, X)\right) \right| \right] \left(K + \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \bar {w} _ {3} \left(U _ {3}\right) \right|\right)\right) \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \left| \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} \left(Y, \hat {y} ^ {*} (t, X)\right) \right| \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

The proof concludes once we show that  $\mathbb{E}_Z\left[|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^* (t,X))|]\right]\to 0$  as  $t\to \infty$

For a fixed  $x$ , let us write  $\mathcal{L}(t,x) = \mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^{*}(t,X))|X = x\right]$  and  $\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t,x) = \mathbb{E}\left[\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^* (t,X))|X = x\right]$  for brevity. Consider Case 1. We claim that if there is an increasing sequence of time  $t_i$  so that  $\lim_{i\to \infty}[\mathcal{L}(t_i,x) - \inf_{\hat{y}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y})|X = x]] = 0$ , then  $\lim_{i\to \infty}|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_i,x)| = 0$ . Indeed, it suffices to show that for any subsequence  $t_{i_j}$  of  $t_i$ , there exists a further subsequence  $t_{i_{j_k}}$  such that  $\lim_{k\to \infty}\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_{j_k}},x)\right| = 0$ . In any subsequence  $t_{i_j}$  of  $t_i$ , using that  $\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},x)$  is convergent and the fact  $\mathcal{L}(y,\hat{y})\rightarrow \infty$  as  $|\hat{y} |\rightarrow \infty$ , we have  $\hat{y}^{*}(t_{i_j},x)$  is bounded. Hence, we obtain a subsequence  $t_{i_{j_k}}$  for which  $\hat{y}^{*}(t_{i_{j_k}},x)$  converges to some limit  $\hat{y}^{*}$ . By continuity, we have  $\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^{*})|X = x] = \lim_{k\to \infty}\mathcal{L}(t_{i_{j_k}},x) = \inf_{\hat{y}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y})|X = x]$ . Thus, since  $\mathcal{L}$  is convex in the second variable, we have  $\mathbb{E}[\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^*)|X = x] = 0$ . Thus,  $\lim_{k\to \infty}\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_{j_k}},x)\right| = |\mathbb{E}[\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y}^*)|X = x]| = 0$ , as claimed. Similarly, we obtain in Case 2 that if there is an increasing sequence of time  $t_i$  so that  $\lim_{i\to \infty}\mathcal{L}(t_i,x) = 0$ , then  $\lim_{i\to \infty}|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_i,x)| = 0$ .

To show that  $\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t,X)\right|\right] \to 0$  as  $t \to \infty$ , it suffices to show that for any increasing sequence of times  $t_i$  tending to infinity, there exists a subsequence  $t_{i_j}$  of  $t_i$  such that  $\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},X)\right|\right] \to 0$ . In Case 1, we have  $\lim_{i\to \infty}\mathcal{L}(W^{*}(t_{i})) = \inf_{\tilde{y}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\mathcal{L}(Y,\tilde{y}(X))\right]$ , so  $\lim_{i\to \infty}\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\mathcal{L}(t_i,X) - \inf_{\tilde{y} (X)}\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\mathcal{L}(Y,\tilde{y}(X))|X\right]\right] = 0$ . Since  $\mathcal{L}(t_i,X) - \inf_{\tilde{y} (X)}\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\mathcal{L}(Y,\tilde{y}(X))|X\right]$  is nonnegative, it converges to 0 in probability. Thus, there is a further subsequence  $t_{i_j}$  for which  $\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},X) - \inf_{\tilde{y} (X)}\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\mathcal{L}(Y,\tilde{y}(X))|X\right]$  converges to 0  $\mathcal{P}$ -almost surely. By the previous claim,  $\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},X)\right|$  converges to 0  $\mathcal{P}$ -almost surely. Since  $\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},X)\right|$  is bounded  $\mathcal{P}$ -almost surely, we obtain that  $\mathbb{E}_Z\left[\left|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(t_{i_j},X)\right|\right] \to 0$  from the bounded convergence theorem. The result in Case 2 can be established similarly.

# F Remaining proofs for Section 7

# F.1 Proof of Proposition 39

Proof of Proposition 39. We recall

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {L} (t, c _ {L - 1}, 1) = - \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; W (t))) \varphi_ {L} ^ {\prime} (H _ {L} (X, 1; W (t))) \varphi_ {L - 1} (H _ {L - 1} (X, c _ {L - 1}; W (t))) \right],
$$

for  $c_{L - 1}\in \Omega_{L - 1}$  . By the regularity assumption,

$$
\left| \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {L} (t, c _ {L - 1}, 1) \right| \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ | \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (X; W (t))) | \right].
$$

Note that the right-hand side is independent of  $c_{L-1}$ . Then as argued in the proof of Proposition 36 (Section E.2),  $\mathbb{E}_Z[|\partial_2\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y} (X;W(t)))|]\to 0$  as  $t\to \infty$ . This completes the proof.

# G Remaining proofs for Section 8

# G.1 Proof of Theorem 41

Proof of Theorem 41. For brevity, let us write

$$
\overline {{H}} _ {2} (x) = H _ {2} (x, 1; \overline {{W}}), \qquad \bar {y} (x) = \hat {y} (x; \overline {{W}}).
$$

We also define

$$
\begin{array}{l} G _ {2} (t, u _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(H _ {2} (t, X, 1)\right) \varphi_ {1} \left(\langle u _ {1}, X \rangle\right) \right], \\ G _ {1} (t, u _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (H _ {2} (t, X, 1)) \varphi_ {1} ^ {\prime} (\langle u _ {1}, X \rangle) X \right], \\ \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \bar {y} (X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (\bar {H} _ {2} (X)) \varphi_ {1} (\langle u _ {1}, X \rangle) \right], \\ \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \overline {{y}} (X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (\overline {{H}} _ {2} (X)) \varphi_ {1} ^ {\prime} (\langle u _ {1}, X \rangle) X \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We claim that as  $t\to \infty$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[\left| H _ {2} (t, X, 1) - \bar {H} _ {2} (X) \right|\right]\rightarrow 0, \quad \mathbb {E} \left[\left| \hat {y} (t, X) - \bar {y} (X) \right|\right]\rightarrow 0, \\ \mathbb {E} \left[ | \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) - \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \overline {{y}} (X)) | \right]\rightarrow 0, \\ \end{array}
$$

and uniformly in  $u_{1}$

$$
\left| G _ {1} (t, u _ {1}) - \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right|\rightarrow 0, \qquad \left| G _ {2} (t, u _ {1}) - \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \right|\rightarrow 0.
$$

Indeed recall the coupling  $\pi_t$  in Assumption 9, we have from Assumption 6.3:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {X} \left[ \left| H _ {2} (t, X, 1) - \overline {{H}} _ {2} (X) \right| \right] = \mathbb {E} _ {X} \left[ \left| \mathbb {E} _ {\left(C _ {1}, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) \sim \pi_ {t}} \left[ w _ {2} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) \varphi_ {1} (\langle w _ {1} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}), X \rangle) - \overline {{w}} _ {2} (C _ {1}) \varphi_ {1} (\langle \overline {{w}} _ {1} (C _ {1}), X \rangle) \right] \right| \right] \\ \leq K \mathbb {E} _ {\pi_ {t}} \left[ | \overline {{w}} _ {2} (C _ {1}) | \left| w _ {1} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}) - \overline {{w}} _ {1} (C _ {1}) \right| + \left| w _ {2} (t, C _ {1} ^ {\prime}, 1) - \overline {{w}} _ {2} (C _ {1}) \right| \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

which tends to 0 as  $t \to \infty$  by Assumption 9. The other claims can be derived similarly.

Consider the limit potential  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  given by

$$
\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1}) = \frac {1}{2} \left| \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \right| ^ {2}.
$$

By Assumption 6.3,  $u_{1} \mapsto \overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_{1})$  is continuous. Notice that

$$
\nabla \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1}) = \frac {1}{2} \cdot 2 \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \nabla \left(\overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1})\right) = \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}).
$$

Let  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}:\mathbb{S}^{d - 1}\to \mathbb{R}$  be defined by  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}(\tilde{u}_1) = \lim_{r\to \infty}\overline{\mathcal{F}} (r\tilde{u}_1)$ , which exists by Assumption 9. We shall argue that  $\overline{\mathcal{F}} (u_1) = 0$  for all  $u_{1}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$ , by contradiction. To that end, let us assume that  $\overline{\mathcal{F}} (u_1)\neq 0$  for some  $u_{1}$ . Note that  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  is bounded by a constant by Assumption 6.3. Thus, either there is a local maximizer  $u_{1}^{*}$  of  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  with  $\overline{\mathcal{F}} (u_1^*) > 0$  or there is a local maximizer  $\tilde{u}_1^*$  of  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}$  with  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}(\tilde{u}_1^*) > 0$ .

First consider the case that  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  has a local maximizer  $u_{1}^{*}$  with  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) > 0$ . Under Assumption 9, there exists  $\delta \in (0, \overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*))$  arbitrarily small so that for  $S_{\delta}$  the connected component of the set  $\{u : \overline{\mathcal{F}}(u) > \overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) - \delta\}$  that contains  $u_{1}^{*}$ , there is  $\xi > 0$  such that  $|\nabla \overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1)| > \xi$  for all  $u_1 \in \partial \operatorname{cl}(S_{\delta})$ . Let  $T_0$  be sufficiently large so that for  $t \geq T_0$ , we have if  $u_1 \in \partial \operatorname{cl}(S_{\delta})$ ,  $|\overline{G}_1(u_1) - G_1(t, u_1)| \leq \xi / \sqrt{8\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*)}$ , which implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}), G _ {1} (t, u _ {1}) \right\rangle \geq \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| ^ {2} - \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) - G _ {1} (t, u _ {1}) \right| \\ \geq \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| ^ {2} - \frac {\xi}{\sqrt {8 \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*})}} \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\geq} \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| ^ {2} - \frac {\left| \nabla \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1}) \right|}{2 \left| \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \right|} \left| \overline {{G}} _ {1} (u _ {1}) \right| \\ = \frac {\left| \nabla \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1}) \right| ^ {2}}{4 \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1})} \\ > \frac {\xi^ {2}}{4 \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} \left(u _ {1} ^ {*}\right)}, \tag {20} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(a)$  is because  $2\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) > 2\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1) = \left|\overline{G}_2(u_1)\right|^2$  for any  $u_1 \in \partial \operatorname{cl}(S_\delta)$  by local maximality of  $u_1^*$  and continuity of  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$ . Also, we further enlarge  $T_0$  so that for  $t \geq T_0$  and any  $u_1 \in \operatorname{cl}(S_\delta)$ ,  $\left|\overline{G}_2(u_1) - G_2(t,u_1)\right| \leq \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) - \delta}$  and hence

$$
G _ {2} (t, u _ {1}) \geq \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) - \frac {1}{2} \sqrt {\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} \left(u _ {1} ^ {*}\right) - \delta} > \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) - \frac {1}{2} \sqrt {\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} \left(u _ {1}\right)} = \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) - \frac {1}{2} \left| \overline {{G}} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \right|, \tag {21}
$$

$$
G _ {2} (t, u _ {1}) \leq \bar {G} _ {2} (u _ {1}) + \frac {1}{2} \sqrt {\bar {\mathcal {F}} \left(u _ {1} ^ {*}\right) - \delta} <   \bar {G} _ {2} (u _ {1}) + \frac {1}{2} \sqrt {\bar {\mathcal {F}} (u _ {1})} = \bar {G} _ {2} (u _ {1}) + \frac {1}{2} \left| \bar {G} _ {2} (u _ {1}) \right|. \tag {22}
$$

Furthermore notice that

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} \bar {G} _ {2} \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})\right) = \left\langle \bar {G} _ {1} \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})\right), \frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {1} (t, C _ {1}) \right\rangle = - w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1) \left\langle \bar {G} _ {1} \left(w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})\right), G _ {1} \left(t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})\right) \right\rangle . \tag {23}
$$

Let  $\tilde{\Omega}_1$  be the subset of  $\Omega_1$  consisting of  $c_{1}$  where  $|w_{2}(0,c_{1},1)| < |F(w_{1}(0,c_{1}))| + 1$  for  $F$  given in Assumption 6.2. The proof of Lemma 55 in fact shows that for any  $t\geq 0$  and any open subset  $B$  of  $\mathbb{R}^d$ , there exists a positive mass of  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  such that  $w_{1}(t,C_{1})\in B$ . In the following, we consider  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$ . We further divide the argument into two cases:  $\overline{G}_2(u_1^*) > 0$  and  $\overline{G}_2(u_1^*) < 0$ .

Let us consider the case that  $\overline{G}_2(u_1^*) > 0$ . Then we can choose sufficiently small  $\delta$  such that  $\overline{G}_2(u_1) > 0$  for all  $u_1 \in \operatorname{cl}(S_\delta)$ . Furthermore, consider the scenario that there exists  $T \geq T_0$  such that a positive mass of  $(w_1(T,C_1), w_2(T,C_1,1))$  with  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  has  $w_1(T,C_1) \in S_\delta$  and  $w_2(T,C_1,1) < 0$ . Note that if  $w_1(t,C_1) \in \operatorname{cl}(S_\delta)$ ,

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1) = - G _ {2} (t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) \leq - \left(\overline {{G}} _ {2} (w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) - \frac {1}{2} | \overline {{G}} _ {2} (w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) |\right) <   0
$$

by Eq. (21). Define  $T_{1} = \inf \{t\geq T:w_{1}(t,C_{1})\notin S_{\delta}\}$ . Then  $t\mapsto w_2(t,C_1,1)$  is decreasing on  $t\in [T,T_1)$ . Let us argue that  $T_{1} = \infty$ . Indeed, suppose  $T_{1}$  is finite. We then have, by continuity,  $w_{1}(T_{1},C_{1})\in \partial \operatorname {cl}(S_{\delta})$  and  $w_{2}(T_{1},C_{1},1)\leq w_{2}(T,C_{1},1) < 0$ . As such,  $\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\overline{G}_2(w_1(T_1,C_1)) > 0$  by Eq. (20) and (23). By continuity,

for some  $\gamma > 0$ ,  $\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\overline{G}_2(w_1(T_1 + t,C_1)) > 0$  for all  $t \in [0,\gamma]$ . But then  $\overline{G}_2(w_1(T_1 + t,C_1)) \geq \overline{G}_2(w_1(T_1,C_1)) \geq \sqrt{2(\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) - \delta)}$ , and hence  $w_1(T_1 + t,C_1) \in S_\delta$  for all  $t \leq \gamma$ , contradicting the definition of  $T_1$ . Therefore  $T_1 = \infty$ , i.e. for  $t \geq T$  and  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  with  $w_1(T,C_1) \in S_\delta$  and  $w_2(T,C_1,1) < 0$ , we have  $w_1(t,C_1) \in S_\delta$ , which implies

$$
G _ {2} (t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) \overset {(a)} {\geq} \frac {1}{2} \overline {{G}} _ {2} (w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) = \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (w _ {1} (t , C _ {1}))} \geq \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} (\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*}) - \delta)},
$$

where  $(a)$  is by Eq. (21) and the fact  $\overline{G}_2(u_1) > 0$  for all  $u_1 \in \operatorname{cl}(S_\delta)$ . In particular, there is a positive mass of  $(w_1(t,C_1), w_2(t,C_1,1))$  with  $G_2(t,w_1(t,C_1)) \geq \sqrt{(\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) - \delta)/2}$  for all  $t \geq T$ . Noting that

$$
\frac {d}{d t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathcal {L} (Y, \hat {y} (t, X)) \right] = - \mathbb {E} \left[ | G _ {2} (t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) | ^ {2} \right] - \mathbb {E} \left[ | G _ {1} (t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) | ^ {2} \right] \leq - \mathbb {E} \left[ | G _ {2} (t, w _ {1} (t, C _ {1})) | ^ {2} \right],
$$

we obtain  $\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}(Y,\hat{y} (t,X))]$  being bounded above by a strictly negative constant for all  $t\geq T$ , which is a contradiction since  $\mathcal{L}$  is bounded below.

Next consider the scenario that for all  $t \geq T_0$ , the probability that  $w_{1}(t,C_{1}) \in S_{\delta}$  and  $w_{2}(t,C_{1},1) < 0$  on  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  is zero. Let us argue that for any  $t \geq T_0$  and for a.e.  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  with  $w_{1}(t,C_{1}) \in S_{\delta}$ , we have  $w_{1}(s,C_{1}) \in S_{\delta}$  for all  $s \in [T_0,t]$ . Indeed, consider  $t$  and  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  such that  $w_{1}(t,C_{1}) \in S_{\delta}$  and  $w_{1}(T',C_{1}) \notin S_{\delta}$  for some  $T' \in [T_0,t)$ . Let  $t' = \sup \{s \in [T',t] : w_{1}(s,C_{1}) \notin S_{\delta}\} < t$ . By continuity,  $w_{1}(t',C_{1}) \in \partial \operatorname{cl}(S_{\delta})$  and so by Eq. (20),

$$
\left\langle \overline {{G}} _ {1} (w _ {1} (t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1})), G _ {1} (t ^ {\prime}, w _ {1} (t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1})) \right\rangle > \frac {\xi^ {2}}{4 \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*})}.
$$

By continuity, there exists  $t'' \in (t', t)$  such that for all  $s \in [t', t'']$ ,

$$
\left\langle \overline {{G}} _ {1} \left(w _ {1} (s, C _ {1})\right), G _ {1} \left(s, w _ {1} (s, C _ {1})\right) \right\rangle \geq \frac {\xi^ {2}}{1 0 0 \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} \left(u _ {1} ^ {*}\right)}.
$$

By definition of  $t'$ , we also have  $w_{1}(s,C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$  and therefore  $w_{2}(s,C_{1},1)\geq 0$  for any  $s\in (t',t]$ . Then by Eq. (23),  $\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\overline{G}_2(w_1(s,C_1))\leq 0$  for all  $s\in (t',t'']$  and therefore

$$
\overline {{G}} _ {2} \left(w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime \prime}, C _ {1}\right)\right) \leq \overline {{G}} _ {2} \left(w _ {1} \left(t ^ {\prime}, C _ {1}\right)\right) = \sqrt {2 \left(\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} \left(u _ {1} ^ {*}\right) - \delta\right)},
$$

where the equality follows from  $w_{1}(t^{\prime},C_{1})\in \partial \operatorname {cl}(S_{\delta})$ . However this contradicts with  $w_{1}(t^{\prime \prime},C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$ . Therefore it holds that for any  $t\geq T_0$ , for a.e.  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  with  $w_{1}(t,C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$ ,  $w_{1}(s,C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$  and therefore  $w_{2}(s,C_{1},1)\geq 0$  for all  $s\in [T_0,t]$ . Since  $w_{1}(t,C_{1})$  on  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  has full support at any  $t\geq 0$ , we have for any  $t_0\geq T_0$ , there is a positive mass on  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  such that  $w_{1}(t_{0},C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$  and hence, as shown,  $w_{1}(s,C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$  and  $w_{2}(s,C_{1},1)\geq 0$  for all  $s\in [T_0,t_0]$ . Note that we have  $w_{2}(T_{0},C_{1},1)\leq M(T_{0})$  for some finite  $M(T_0) > 0$  for  $C_1\in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  (which follows from the fact  $\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t} w_2(t,\cdot ,1)\right|\leq K$  by Assumption 6.3 and that  $|w_{2}(0,C_{1},1)| < |F(w_{1}(0,C_{1}))| + 1\leq K)$ . Also note that for  $w_{1}(s,C_{1})\in S_{\delta}$  and  $s\geq T_0$ ,

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} w _ {2} (s, C _ {1}, 1) = - G _ {2} (s, w _ {1} (s, C _ {1})) \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} - \frac {1}{2} \overline {{G}} _ {2} (w _ {1} (s, C _ {1})) = - \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} \overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (w _ {1} (s , C _ {1}))} \leq - \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} (\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*}) - \delta)}
$$

a strictly negative constant, where  $(a)$  is by Eq. (21) and the fact  $\overline{G}_2(u_1) > 0$  for all  $u_{1}\in \operatorname {cl}(S_{\delta})$ . As such, for any  $t_0\geq T_0$  such that

$$
M \left(T _ {0}\right) - \left(t _ {0} - T _ {0}\right) \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} \left(\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*}) - \delta\right)} <   0,
$$

there is a positive mass on  $C_1 \in \tilde{\Omega}_1$  such that firstly  $w_{2}(s,C_{1},1) \geq 0$  for all  $s \in [T_0,t_0]$  and secondly there exists  $t \in [T_0,t_0]$  in which

$$
w _ {2} (t, C _ {1}, 1) \leq M (T _ {0}) - (t - T _ {0}) \sqrt {\frac {1}{2} (\overline {{\mathcal {F}}} (u _ {1} ^ {*}) - \delta)} <   0.
$$

We again obtain a contradiction.

The case  $\overline{G}_2(u_1^*) < 0$  can be treated similarly, with the use of Eq. (21) replaced by Eq. (22). Both cases lead to a contradiction, ruling out the possibility that there is a local maximizer  $u_1^*$  of  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  with  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1^*) > 0$ .

Next consider the case  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}$  does not have any local maximizer in  $\mathbb{R}^d$  but  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^\infty$  has a local maximizer  $\tilde{u}_1^*$  with  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}^\infty (\tilde{u}_1^*) > 0$ . Under Assumption 9 (and with the same argument in the discussion that follows), there exists  $\delta \in \left(0,\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}\left(\tilde{u}_{1}^{*}\right)\right)$  arbitrarily small so that for  $S_{\delta}$  the connected component of the set  $\{u\in \mathbb{R}^d:\overline{\mathcal{F}} (u) > \overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}(\tilde{u}_{1}^{*}) - \delta \}$  which contains  $r\tilde{u}_1^*$  for all  $r$  sufficiently large, there is  $\xi >0$  such that  $|\nabla \overline{\mathcal{F}} (u)| > \xi$  for all  $u\in \partial \operatorname {cl}(S_{\delta})$ . The rest of the argument can be repeated as before to yield a contradiction.

In short, we have shown that  $\overline{\mathcal{F}}(u_1) = \frac{1}{2} \left| \overline{G}_2(u_1) \right|^2 = 0$ , and equivalently,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {Z} \left[ \partial_ {2} \mathcal {L} (Y, \bar {y} (X)) \varphi_ {2} ^ {\prime} (\bar {H} _ {2} (X)) \varphi_ {1} (\langle u _ {1}, X \rangle) \right] = 0
$$

for all  $u_{1}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$ . The remaining proof follows identically as in the proof of Theorem 31.
